Trace and Index of Dirac-Schrödinger Operators on Open Space with Operator Potentials
Oliver Fürst

Abstract
We develop a principal trace and generalized index formula for a Dirac-Schrödinger operator D D on open space of odd dimension d ≥ 3 d\geq 3 with a potential given by a family of self-adjoint unbounded operators acting on a infinite dimensional Hilbert space H H . The presented results generalize formulas surrounding the Callias index theorem to the case of unbounded operator potentials, for which the operator D D is not necessarily Fredholm. This is the principal novelty of this paper. As application, we include examples where the trace formula is used to calculate the Witten index of non-Fredholm massless ( d + 1 ) (d+1) -Dirac-Schrödinger operators acting in L 2 ​ ( ℝ d + 1 , H ) L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d+1},H\right) .



1
Introduction

The central goal of this paper is to calculate the trace (called in the following the ”principal trace”)
\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)
,
t>0
, of the Dirac-Schrödinger operator
D=\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+A
, and the associated index formula, under suitable conditions on the operator-valued potential
A
.

Let us start with the setup, explaining the notation above. We fix
\mathbbm{i}
as the imaginary unit in
\mathbb{C}
. Let
H
be a separable complex Hilbert space, and let
A_{0}
be a self-adjoint operator in
H
with domain
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, which plays the role of a ”model operator”. We equip domains of (Borel) functions
f
of
A_{0}
with the corresponding graph norm, which turns
\mathrm{Dom}\ f\left(A_{0}\right)
into a separable Hilbert space, embedding continuously into
H
with norm
1
. Fix
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\geq 3
to be the odd dimension of the euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
A
be a function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with values in the self-adjoint operators in
H
with constant domain
\mathrm{Dom}\ A\left(x\right)=\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Denote by
M_{0}
the constant operator function
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto A_{0}
. Fix
r\in\mathbb{N}
as the rank (the minimal choice is
r=2^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
) of Clifford matrices
\left(c^{i}\right)_{i=1}^{d}\subset\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
, i.e. for
i,j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
,

\displaystyle c^{i}c^{j}+c^{j}c^{i}=-2\delta_{ij}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}.

(1)

For
p\in\left[1,\infty\right]
denote by
L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
the
\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H
-valued
L^{p}
elements, and with slight abuse of notation, denote by
A
and
M_{0}
the multiplication operators in the Hilbert space
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
, given by

\displaystyle\left(Af\right)\left(x\right):=

\displaystyle A\left(x\right)f\left(x\right),\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ f\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A,

\displaystyle\left(M_{0}f\right)\left(x\right):=

\displaystyle A_{0}f\left(x\right),\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ f\in\mathrm{Dom}\ M_{0},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dom}\ A:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dom}\ M_{0}:=L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}\right).

(2)

Denote by
H^{k}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
the
L^{2}
-Sobolev space of order
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with values in
\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H
. Let
\not{\partial}
be the Dirac operator in
\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H
given by

\displaystyle\left(\not{\partial}f\right)\left(x\right):=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}c^{i}\left(\partial_{i}f\right)\left(x\right),\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ f\in\mathrm{Dom}\ \not{\partial},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dom}\ \not{\partial}:=

\displaystyle H^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right).

(3)

It is well-known that
\not{\partial}
is self-adjoint, with
\Delta=\not{\partial}^{2}=\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H}
, where
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is the self-adjoint euclidean Laplace operator
\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}:=-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\partial_{i}^{2}
,
\mathrm{Dom}\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}:=H^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
. In
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
introduce the operators
D,D_{0}
given by

\displaystyle D:=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+A,\ D_{0}:=\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+M_{0},

(4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dom}\ D=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{0}=\mathrm{Dom}\ \not{\partial}\cap\mathrm{Dom}\ M_{0}.

(5)

D_{0}
is normal with
D_{0}^{\ast}=-\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+M_{0}
, and
\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{0}^{\ast}=\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{0}
(cf.
[
25
]
[Lemma 4.2]). We introduce the non-negative operator
H_{0}
given by

\displaystyle H_{0}:=

\displaystyle D_{0}D_{0}^{\ast}=D_{0}^{\ast}D_{0}=\Delta+M_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}=

\displaystyle H^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\cap\mathrm{Dom}\ M_{0}^{2}.

(6)

In the following preparatory section we will give conditions on
A
, such that the domain conditions
\mathrm{Dom}\ DD^{\ast}=\mathrm{Dom}\ D^{\ast}D=\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
,
\mathrm{Dom}\ D=\mathrm{Dom}\ D^{\ast}=\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{0}
are satisfied. We will also show that under further assumptions on the function
A
, akin to the asymptotic homogeneity condition imposed classically in Callias original paper (
[
13
]
[Theorem 2]), the partial trace
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)
is trace-class in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
, for
t>0
.
After introducing a suitable approximation of the operator function
A
, the bulk of this paper then deals with the calculation of the principal trace.

Let us now present the conditions we impose on the operator function
A=B+M_{0}
, and discuss some of the details. We use the following notation:
\langle x\rangle_{z}:=\left(\left|x\right|^{2}+z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and
\langle x\rangle:=\langle x\rangle_{1}
.

Hypothesis 1.1

(Hypothesis
2.11
)
.

Let
\alpha\geq 1
, and
N\geq\lfloor\frac{\alpha-1}{2}\left(d+1\right)\rfloor+1
. Let
B
be a family of symmetric operators in
H
, with domains
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
. Assume that
B\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}
is continuously differentiable in the strong operator topology, and that for all
\beta\in\left[-2N-1,2N+1\right]
the operators
\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}B\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1-\beta}
are closable for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with bounded closures and

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta}B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1-\beta}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}<\infty.

(7)

Assume that for
\beta=\pm 2N
,

\displaystyle\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta}B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{1+\beta}B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1-\beta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle+\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\nabla B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1-\beta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)^{d}\right)}\xrightarrow{w\to\infty}0,\ w:=\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right).

(8)

Further assume that for all
\beta\in\left[-2N+\alpha,2N+1\right]
, there exists
\epsilon>0
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|x\mapsto\langle x\rangle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(\nabla B\right)\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{d}\left(H\right)^{d}\right)}

\displaystyle+\left\|x\mapsto\langle x\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)}<\infty,

(9)

where
\partial_{R}:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{x^{i}}{\left|x\right|}\partial_{i}
denotes the radial vector field, and
S^{d}\left(H\right)
the
d
th Schatten-von Neumann class of
H
.

Finally, let
S_{1}\left(0\right)
denote the
\left(d-1\right)
-dimensional unit sphere, and assume that for
\phi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R\left\|\left(\nabla B\left(Ry+x\right)-\nabla B\left(Ry\right)\right)\phi\right\|_{H^{d}}=\lim_{R\to\infty}\left\|\left(B\left(Ry+x\right)-B\left(Ry\right)\right)\phi\right\|_{H}=0.

(10)

Let us give an overview over the above conditions. We first note that the parameter
\alpha\geq 1
is central. It allows flexibility in the most important condition (
1.1
) where a larger
\alpha
means a weaker requirement on
B
. It should be noted however that this comes at a price, namely that
N
has to be increased. The parameter
N
determines how regular
B
needs to be on the Hilbert scale generated by
A_{0}
(cf. conditions (
7
) and (
1.1
). It should however be noted that the regularity of
B
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
needs to be in any case just once continuously differentiable, independent of the choice of
\alpha
, and thus
N
.
The condition (
1.1
) encodes Kato-Rellich bounds on the family
B
, which in particular imply that all
A\left(x\right)=B\left(x\right)+A_{0}
have constant domain
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
. In rough terms condition (
1.1
) ensures that the domain properties of the involved operators are well-behaved.
The condition (
1.1
) stipulates the (relative) Schatten-von Neumann membership of the derivatives of the operator function
B
and their decay for
\left|x\right|\to\infty
. The radial derivative is singled out, since we assume that it decays faster. These decay conditions correspond to the assumption of asymptotic homogeneity of order
0
in Callias’ classical setup
[
13
]
[Theorem 2]. The final condition (
10
) also refers to the behaviour of
B
at infinity. Roughly speaking, it implies that it should not matter on which ray one approaches a fixed direction at infinity of the functions
B
and
\nabla B
.

It should be remarked that while the above conditions are quite general, one should also emphasize that the possibility of arbitrarily choosing
\alpha\geq 1
is not only interesting for generality but also because of the requirements from applications. If one wants to incorporate the choice of a proper differential operator of order
1
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
for
A_{0}
itself (
H
is then
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)
), one runs into problems with condition (
1.1
) if one is just allowed to take
\alpha=1
. Due to well-known Schatten-von Neumann estimates (cf. Chapter 4 in
[
35
]
) this issue boils basically down to the fact that
x^{-n}
is not integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
at infinity. We note that at the end of this paper we include as an example results dealing with exactly this sketched setup, which stem from
[
24
]
, which gives the detailed proofs in this particular setup, which we omit in this paper.

After explaining the conditions imposed on the operator potential
A
, let us present the main result of this paper, which is the desired principal trace formula in terms of
A
and
{\rm d}A
in a neighbourhood of infinity.

Theorem 1.2

(Theorem
6.5
)
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
. For
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
denote
A_{\phi}:=A_{0}+\left(1-\phi\right)\left(A-A_{0}\right)
. Then for
t>0
, and any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left({\rm d}A_{\phi}e^{-ts_{j}A_{\phi}^{2}}\right){\rm d}s,

(11)

where (
1.2
) is independent of the choice of
\phi
, and

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}:=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(c^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot c^{d}\right),

(12)

\Delta_{d-1}=\left\{u\in\left[0,1\right]^{d},\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}u_{j}=1\right\}
denotes the
\left(d-1\right)
-simplex, and
\bigwedge
the exterior product.

We note that formula (
1.2
) only depends on the values of
A
and
{\rm d}A
in a neighbourhood of infinity due to the cut-off
\phi
. If
A
and
{\rm d}A
additionally assume radial limits in a particular sense, we show that only these limits appear in the trace formula.

Corollary 1.3

(Corollary
6.6
)
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
, and denote
A^{\circ}\left(y\right)\psi:=\lim_{R\to\infty}A\left(Ry\right)\psi
, for
\psi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, and
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
. Assume that
A^{\circ}\psi
is differentiable on
S_{1}\left(0\right)
for
\psi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, such that for a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R\left\|\partial_{i}A^{\circ}\left(y\right)\psi-\partial_{i}A\left(Ry\right)\psi\right\|_{H}=0.

(13)

and that for
\beta\in\left[-2N+\alpha,2N+1\right]
, and all unit vector fields
\nu
in
TS_{1}\left(0\right)
,

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(\nu A^{\circ}\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\in L^{\infty}\left(S_{1}\left(0\right),S^{d}\left(H\right)\right).

(14)

Denote for
\rho\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle:=A_{0}+\left(1-\rho\left(\left|x\right|\right)\right)\left(A^{\circ}\left(\frac{x}{\left|x\right|}\right)-A_{0}\right).

(15)

Then for
t>0
, and any
\rho\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
, with
\rho\equiv 1
near
0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}\right)e^{-ts_{j}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}^{2}}\right){\rm d}u,

(16)

independent of the choice of
\rho
.

As a direct application of the principal trace formula of Theorem
1.2
we may calculate the Fredholm index of
D
, whenever
D
is Fredholm.

Theorem 1.4

(Theorem
7.3
)
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
. Assume that there exists
R_{0}\geq 0
, such that for
\left|x\right|\geq R_{0}
, we have
0\in\rho\left(A\left(x\right)\right)
. Then
D
is Fredholm, and the Fredholm index
\ind D
is given by

\displaystyle\ind D=\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}\lim_{t\to\infty}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi}\right)e^{-ts_{j}A_{\phi}^{2}}\right){\rm d}s,

(17)

where
A_{\phi}:=A_{0}+\left(1-\phi\right)\left(A-A_{0}\right)
for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
. Formula (
17
) is independent of the choice of
\phi
.

The principal trace formula of Theorem
1.2
, can also be applied to determine some cases a generalized version of index, the so called (partial) Witten index, of the operator
D
. The notion was introduced in
[
27
]
. We give here the definition of the semi-group regularized partial Witten index.

Definition 1.5
.

Let
T
be a linear, densely defined operator
\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes X
for some separable complex Hilbert space
X
. We say the partial (semi-group regularized) Witten index of
T
exists, if for some
t_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tT^{\ast}T}-e^{-tTT^{\ast}}\right)\in S^{1}\left(X\right),\ t\geq t_{0},

(18)

and if the limit

\displaystyle\ind_{W}T:=\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{X}\left(\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tT^{\ast}T}-e^{-tTT^{\ast}}\right)\right),

(19)

exists, then
\ind_{W}T
is called the partial Witten index of
T
.

The (partial) Witten index may assume any real number (cf.
[
7
]
,
[
14
]
,
[
16
]
in dimension
d=1
, and
[
24
]
for
d\geq 3
), however if
T
is additionally Fredholm, it coincides with the usual Fredholm index (see Remark
7.2
).

We conclude this paper, as we mentioned before, with examples of partial Witten index formulas taken from
[
24
]
without their proofs, dealing with massless
\left(d+1\right)
-Dirac-Schrödinger operators. These operators are non-Fredholm, but possess partial Witten index formulas given in Example
7.5
, and for a special choice of potential in Example
7.7
. The point of these examples is that they extend the known Witten index formula, given directly in terms of the underlying potential, in the case
d=1
(cf.
[
7
]
,
[
14
]
,
[
16
]
), to
d\geq 3
odd. In this presented setup the model operator
A_{0}=\mathbbm{i}\partial
is just the derivative in one direction, and
B
is given by a (matrix) potential over
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
.

Definition 1.6
.

For a potential
V:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow B_{sa}\left(H\right)
, define the associated
massless
\left(d+1\right)
-Dirac-Schrödinger operator

D_{V}
,

\displaystyle\left(D_{V}f\right)\left(x,y\right):=

\displaystyle i\sum_{j=1}^{d}c^{j}\partial_{x^{j}}f\left(x,y\right)+i\partial_{y}f\left(x,y\right)+V\left(x,y\right)f\left(x,y\right),\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ y\in\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle f\in

\displaystyle W^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d+1},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right),

(20)

Without going into the details of the assumptions on the potential
V
, we then have the index formula:

Example 1.7

(Example
7.5
)
.

Assume that
V
satisfies the requirements given in Example
7.5
. Then

\displaystyle\ind_{W}D_{V}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{d-1}{2}\right)!}{d!}\kappa_{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\left(U^{V}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}U^{V}\right)^{\wedge d},

(21)

where
\wedge d
is the
d
-fold exterior power,

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}:=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(c^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot c^{d}\right),

(22)

and the unitary
U^{V}
is given by
U^{V}\left(x\right):=\lim_{n\to\infty}U^{V\left(x,\cdot\right)}\left(n,-n\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)
,
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, for a given
A:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow B_{sa}\left(H\right)
is the (unique) evolutions system of the equation

\displaystyle u^{\prime}\left(y\right)=\mathbbm{i}A\left(y\right)u\left(y\right),\ y\in\mathbb{R}.

(23)

Before we proceed, let us outline the historical context of the problem. A differential operator of the type
D=\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+A
was discussed prominently by Atiyah, Patodi and Singer in their seminal series of papers
[
3
]
[
4
]
[
5
]
. Therein
A
is a family over
\mathbb{R}
of first order, elliptic, differential operators on a compact, odd dimensional manifold with invertible asymptotic endpoints. They showed that
D
is Fredholm with Fredholm index
\ind D=\mathrm{sflow}\left(A,0\right)
, where the right hand side is the spectral flow of
A
through
0
. The one-dimensional case, in which
A
is parametrized over
\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}
is just the derivative along
\mathbb{R}
, was further discussed by Robbin and Salamon
[
34
]
for more general types of unbounded operator families
A
, however still with
A\left(x\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
, having discrete spectra. One should also note
[
7
]
, where some of the notions about generalized index, and spectral flow, appeared. In 2008, Pushnitski showed in
[
32
]
that discreteness of the spectrum of
A
can, in essence, be replaced by the following assumption: Assume that
A=A_{-}+B
, where
A_{-}
is a generic self-adjoint operator in a separable Hilbert space, and the derivative
B^{\prime}
of the family
B
of bounded, symmetric operators is an integrable family of trace-class operators in
H
, i.e.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|B^{\prime}\left(x\right)\right\|_{S^{1}\left(H\right)}<\infty.

(24)

Under these conditions
D
is Fredholm with

\displaystyle\ind D=\xi\left(0,A_{+},A_{-}\right),

(25)

where
A_{\pm}=\lim_{x\to\pm\infty}A\left(x\right)
are the (self-adjoint) limit endpoints in a certain sense, and
\xi\left(\cdot,A_{+},A_{-}\right)
is the associated spectral shift function, a concept from scattering theory (cf.
[
38
]
) which we will not delve into here, however it should be noted, that if the spectral shift function of the endpoints and the spectral flow of a (regular enough) family exist, then they coincide (cf. also
[
7
]
). However Pushnitski’s results go beyond a generalization of the index problem. In fact, a principal trace formula (which in general carries more information on
D
than the Fredholm index), already present in Callias paper
[
13
]
, was shown in this more general setup:

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R},H\right)}\left(\left(DD^{\ast}-z\right)^{-1}-\left(D^{\ast}D-z\right)^{-1}\right)=\frac{1}{2z}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(A_{+}\left(A_{+}^{2}-z\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-A_{-}\left(A_{-}^{2}-z\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

(26)

for
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\left[0,\infty\right)
. In the following years, and also quite recently, more results emerged similar to Pushnitski’s trace based approach to the index problem, for example condition (
24
) was replaced with

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|B^{\prime}\left(x\right)\left(A_{-}^{2}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{S^{1}\left(H\right)}<\infty,

(27)

by Gesztesy et al. in
[
25
]
, while (
26
) still holds. Further generalizations along these lines have been discussed in
[
15
]
, and
[
17
]
. We remark that the principal trace formula in these publications (
26
) corresponds to the case
d=1
in the principal trace formula (
1.2
) presented in this paper, if one rewrites the right hand side of (
26
) with an appropriate integral (the endpoints
A_{\pm}
can be seen as the endpoints of the function
A(t)=A_{-}+t(A_{+}-A_{-})
, an application of the fundamental theorem of calculus then yields the integrated form), and turned via the inverse Laplace transform into an exponentiated version instead of the resolvent version (
26
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R},H\right)}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)=\sqrt{\frac{t}{\pi}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(e^{-tA(s)^{2}}\left(A_{+}-A_{-}\right)\right){\rm d}s.

(28)

Another important result, extending the classical ”Index = Spectral Flow” Theorem, was provided by Callias in the paper
[
13
]
. While the operator family
A
acts there in a finite dimensional space, the index formula therein holds for
\not{\partial}
being the classical Dirac operator in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
for
d
odd, which is also our setup here. The index theorem (
[
13
]
[Theorem 2]) then reads as follows: Let
k=\frac{d-1}{2}
. With the minimal rank choice
r=2^{k}
for the Clifford matrices, let
A
be a smooth family of
m\times m
-matrices with
\left|A\left(x\right)\right|\geq C>0
for all
\left|x\right|\geq c
for some constants
c
,
C
. Assume that
A
is asymptotically homogeneous of order
0
as
x\to\infty
(i.e.
A\left(x\right)
is a
0
-order classic symbol in
x
). Then
D
is Fredholm, and, with
U\left(x\right):=A\left(x\right)\left|A\left(x\right)\right|^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\ind D=\frac{1}{2k!}\left(\frac{\mathbbm{i}}{8\pi}\right)^{k}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{S_{R}}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}U\left({\rm d}U\right)^{\wedge\left(d-1\right)},

(29)

here,
S_{R}
denotes the
d-1
-sphere of radius
R
around
0
, and
\left({\rm d}U\right)^{\wedge\left(d-1\right)}
the
d-1
-fold exterior product of (matrix-)one-forms
{\rm d}U
. One should note that there are some problems with the trace-class membership of certain operators in
[
13
]
in the original proof
1
1

1

This may be due to the fact that shortly after Callias published his results a different proof was presented by Bott and Seeley in
[
8
]
, which Callias already mentions in the introduction of his paper.
, however the ideas have been made rigorous (and generalized) by Gesztesy and Waurick in
[
28
]
.

Although beyond the scope of this paper, one should also mention that there exist a multitude of generalizations of Callias’ index theorem to non-compact Riemannian manifolds (cf.
[
1
]
,
[
10
]
,
[
2
]
,
[
33
]
,
[
12
]
,
[
31
]
,
[
18
]
,
[
30
]
,
[
9
]
,
[
37
]
,
[
21
]
,
[
19
]
,
[
36
]
,
[
22
]
), however still in the context of Fredholmness and not providing a general principal trace formula. The usual assumption therein is that the operator valued potential
A
is invertible in some sense outside a compact region of the manifold, to ensure Fredholmness of
D
. We emphasize that we do not assume such a condition in this paper.

With this perspective, the principal trace formula (
1.2
) stands in analogy to (
29
), similar in spirit to the generalizations developed by Pushniski in the one dimensional case for the ”Index=Spectral Flow”-Theorem, especially if one no longer requires
D
to be Fredholm.

2
Trace-class properties of the resolvents of
D^{\ast}D
and
DD^{\ast}

In this section, we present our conditions on the family
A
and show domain, and trace-class properties. We start however by introducing further notation.

Denote by
S^{p}\left(H\right)
the
p
-th Schatten-von Neumann class of
H
, and with
B\left(H\right)
the linear bounded operators of
H
. Denote
\langle T\rangle_{z}:=\left(T^{\ast}T+z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\left(-\infty,0\right]
, and
\langle T\rangle:=\langle T\rangle_{1}
, for an operator
T
in a Hilbert space. Denote for a measurable function
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
g\left(X\right)
the multiplication operator in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
, given by
\left(g\left(X\right)f\right)\left(x\right):=g\left(x\right)f\left(x\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
z\in\rho\left(T\right)
, denote
R_{z}\left(T\right):=\left(T+z\right)^{-1}
.

The following simple relation between trace and Clifford matrices is essential.

Lemma 2.1
.

1.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}c^{\alpha_{j}}\right)=0,

(30)

if (
n
,
d
are odd, and
n<d
) or (
n
is odd, and
d
is even).

2.

For
d
odd, and
\alpha\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}c^{\alpha_{j}}\right)=\kappa_{c}\epsilon_{\alpha},

(31)

where
\epsilon_{\alpha}=\epsilon_{\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{d}}
is the Levi-Civita symbol, and

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}:=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{d}c^{j}\right).

(32)

If
r=2^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
, i.e. the minimal choice of
r
to satisfy the anti commutator relation (
1
), then

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}=\left(2\mathbbm{i}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\left(-\mathbbm{i}\right)^{d}.

(33)

Proof.

1.

Due to the anti commutator relation (
1
), it is sufficient to consider only those
\alpha
for which all
\alpha_{j}
,
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}
, are pairwise different. Since
n
is odd, we have
n<d
in any case. Thus there exists
m\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
such that
m\neq\alpha_{j}
,
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,n\right\}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}c^{\alpha_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left(c^{m}\right)^{2}\prod_{j=1}^{n}c^{\alpha_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(c^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{n}c^{\alpha_{j}}c^{m}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{n+1}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left(c^{m}\right)^{2}\prod_{j=1}^{n}c^{\alpha_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}c^{\alpha_{j}}\right).

(34)

2.

Statement (
31
) follows immediately from the anti commutator relation (
1
), the definition of
\epsilon_{\alpha}
, and the first part of this proof. The case of
r=2^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
follows by induction in
d
and the representation of Clifford matrices of odd dimension
d
by those of dimension
d-2
. The sign convention corresponds to the choice
c^{j}=-\mathbbm{i}\sigma^{j}
, where
\sigma^{j}
are the classical Pauli matrices.

∎

Definition 2.2
.

For
B
a function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with values in symmetric operators in
H
with common domain
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, let
D=D_{B}:=\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+B+M_{0}
,
\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{B}:=\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{0}
, and
H_{B}:=\Delta+\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}
,
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{B}:=\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
.

Definition 2.3
.

For
B
a operator function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with domains
\mathrm{Dom}\ B\left(x\right)=\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, we write
B\in C^{k}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},m\right)
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m\geq 0
, if
\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}B\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1-\beta}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, is a family of closable operators with closures in
B\left(H\right)
for any
\beta\in\left[-m,m\right]
, and
x\mapsto\overline{\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}B\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1-\beta}}\in C^{k}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)
, where
C^{k}_{b}
is the the space of
k
-times continuously differentiable bounded functions in the strong operator topology with bounded derivatives, where boundedness is with respect to the
B\left(H\right)
operator norm. As usual,
C^{k}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
just denotes the scalar/matrix valued-
C^{k}_{b}
-functions. All involved spaces come equipped with the obvious norm given by summing the appropriate
L^{\infty}
-norms of the derivatives up to order
k
.

Definition 2.4
.

We introduce the following semi-norms for operators
B\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},1\right)
, with
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\left(-\infty,0\right]
,

\displaystyle\rho_{z}\left(B\right):=

\displaystyle\left\|B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle+\left\|\nabla B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)^{d}\right)}.

(35)

For
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
denote for
B\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},\left|\beta\right|+1\right)
,

\displaystyle\rho_{z}^{\beta}\left(B\right):=\rho_{z}\left(\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta}B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right).

(36)

Remark 2.5
.

Complex interpolation implies certain operator inequalities (cf
[
26
]
). In particular, by combining
[
26
]
[Theorem 2.8] with Jensen’s inequality, we have for
\beta\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
,

\displaystyle\rho^{\beta}_{z}\left(B\right)\leq\rho^{-2N}_{z}\left(B\right)+\rho^{2N}_{z}\left(B\right).

(37)

Proposition 2.6
.

Let a family
B\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},1\right)
of symmetric operators satisfy

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\to\infty}\rho_{z}\left(B\right)=0.

(38)

Then
D_{B}
, is closed in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
with
D_{B}^{\ast}=-\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+B+M_{0}
,
\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{B}^{\ast}=\mathrm{Dom}\ D_{0}
. Moreover the operators
D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}
,
D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}
, and
H_{B}
are non-negative, and self-adjoint with domain
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof of the statement about
D_{B}
and
D^{\ast}_{B}
is similar to the proof of (
[
25
]
[Lemma 4.4]), although it is assumed that
d=1
, the argument works with the necessary changes for all dimensions
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let us provide at least an outline. The authors show that
B
is relatively bounded by
D_{0}
, and
B^{\ast}
by
D_{0}^{\ast}
, with bound
0
, provided that

\displaystyle\left\|B\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{R}\ni z\to\infty}0.

(39)

This condition is however satisfied by (
38
). The statement then follows from the Kato-Rellich Theorems (
[
29
]
[Theorem 4.1.1, Theorem 5.4.3]). The same idea works for
D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}
and
D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}
, which are self-adjoint by definition. Let us consider
D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}
, the proof for
D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}
is analogous. As differential operators we have
D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}=\Delta+\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}+\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
, which implies

\displaystyle D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}-H_{0}=B^{2}+M_{0}B+BM_{0}+\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right),

(40)

on
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
. We estimate for
z\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(B^{2}+M_{0}B+BM_{0}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}^{E}B\right)R_{z^{2}}\left(H_{0}\right)\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\left\|B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle+\left\|B\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+\left\|\nabla B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)^{d}\right)}\xrightarrow[(\ref{hdomainpropeq1})]{z\to\infty}0.

(41)

Hence
\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}+\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
is relatively bounded by
H_{0}
with bound
0
. Thus
D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}
is self-adjoint on
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
, and essentially self-adjoint on any core of
H_{0}
. Since
C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\otimes\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathcal{D}
is a common core of both
D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}
, and
H_{0}
, we have
\mathrm{Dom}\left(D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}\right)=\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
.

For
H_{B}
we have a similar result, but we have to add that
H_{B}\geq 0
on
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
follows by a direct calculation.
∎

Remark 2.7
.

Under the prerequisites of Proposition
2.6
, it also follows that
A\left(x\right)=A_{0}+B\left(x\right)
is self-adjoint on
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Indeed, since
\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}\langle D_{0}\rangle_{z}^{-1}
is bounded in norm by
1
, uniformly for
z>0
, it follows from
\left\|T\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)}=\left\|T\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}
for measurable multiplication operators
T
, that
B\left(x\right)
is relatively bounded by
A_{0}
with bound
0
for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since
B\left(x\right)
is symmetric, the statement follows from the Kato-Rellich Theorem.

Next, we recall some well-known facts about Schatten-von Neumann operators.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
2\leq p<\infty
,
x\mapsto T\left(x\right)\in L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{p}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, and
g\in L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, then
Tg\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)\in S^{p}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
with

\displaystyle\left\|Tg\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)\right\|_{S^{p}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq\left(2\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{p}}\left\|T\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{p}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}.

(42)

Proof.

The proof of this statement is analogous to the proof of Theorem 4.1 in
[
35
]
. There,
H=\mathbb{C}
, which one has to change here. We give the essential outline. For
p=2
the statement follows from the fact that there is a
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{2}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-valued integral kernel
k
of
Tg\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)
, given by

\displaystyle k\left(x,y\right)=\left(2\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}T\left(x\right)\check{g}\left(x-y\right),\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(43)

where
\check{g}\left(u\right):=\left(2\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}u\cdot\xi}g\left(\xi\right)\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}{\rm d}\xi
. The operator
Tg\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)
is thus Hilbert Schmidt with equality in (
42
). On the other hand,
x\mapsto T\left(x\right)\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
implies that
T\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, and Borel functional calculus implies
g\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|Tg\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq\left\|T\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}.

(44)

If
T
, and
g
are compactly supported the case
p\geq 2
with (
42
) follows from complex interpolation. For general
T
, and
g
let
\left(\phi_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of compactly supported continuous functions, which converge pointwise to
1
on
\mathbb{R}
. Then the self-adjoint operators
\phi_{n}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H}
, and
\phi_{n}\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)
converge strongly to
\mathbbm{1}_{L^{2}\left(E\otimes H\right)}
. With
T_{n}\left(x\right):=\phi_{n}\left(x\right)T\left(x\right)
and
g_{n}:=\phi_{n}g
, we obtain
T_{n}g_{n}\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)=\phi_{n}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H}Tg\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)\phi_{n}\left(\left|\not{\partial}\right|\right)
. By Lemma
2.18
and since
\lim_{n\to\infty}T_{n}=T
in
L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{p}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, and
\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{n}=g
in
L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, the general case follows, by taking the limits past the norms in (
42
).
∎

Corollary 2.9
.

Let
x\mapsto T\left(x\right)\in L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{d}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, such that
T\left(x\right)
is self-adjoint for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then for
t>\frac{d}{p}
we have a constant
C_{d,p}<\infty
, such that,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{s-t}T\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-s}\in

\displaystyle S^{p}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{s-t}T\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-s}\right\|_{S^{p}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq

\displaystyle C_{d,p}\left\|T\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{p}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)},\ s\in\left[0,t\right].

(45)

Proof.

First consider
s=t
. We note that
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi\rangle^{-t}\in L^{p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, so Lemma
2.8
applies. The case
s=0
follows by taking adjoints. The general case follows by complex interpolation (cf.
[
26
]
).
∎

Definition 2.10
.

We introduce a second set of semi-norms for
B\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},m\right)
,
m\geq 0
, and the following data:
\alpha\geq 0
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, with
\left|\beta-1\right|\leq m
,
\left|\beta-\alpha\right|\leq m
,
p\geq 2
,
s\geq 0
, and a unit vector field
\nu\in\Gamma\left(T\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
,

\displaystyle\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nu,p,s}\left(B\right):=\left\|x\mapsto\langle x\rangle^{s}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(\nu B\right)\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{p}\left(H\right)\right)},

(46)

and denote
\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,p,s}:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\partial_{i},p,s}
.

We introduce the set of conditions on the family operator family
A=M_{0}+B
.

Hypothesis 2.11
.

Let
\alpha\geq 1
, and
N\geq\lfloor\frac{\alpha-1}{2}\left(d+1\right)\rfloor+1
. Let
B
be a family of symmetric operators in
H
, with domains
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
. Assume that
B\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},2N+1\right)
. Assume that

1.

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\to\infty}\rho_{z}^{-2N}\left(B\right)+\rho_{z}^{2N}\left(B\right)=0,

(47)

and that for all
\beta\in\left[-2N+\alpha,2N+1\right]
,

\displaystyle\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right)

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\exists\epsilon>0:\ \tau^{\alpha,\beta,\partial_{R},d,1+\epsilon}\left(B\right)

\displaystyle<\infty,

(48)

where
\partial_{R}:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{x^{i}}{\left|x\right|}\partial_{i}
denotes the radial vector field.

2.

Let
S_{1}\left(0\right)
denote the
\left(d-1\right)
-dimensional unit sphere. For
\phi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\left|\gamma\right|\leq 1
, assume,

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R^{\left|\gamma\right|}\left\|\left(\partial^{\gamma}B\left(Ry+x\right)-\partial^{\gamma}B\left(Ry\right)\right)\phi\right\|_{H}

\displaystyle=0.

(49)

Remark 2.12
.

By a Kato-Rellich argument Hypothesis
2.11
implies that for a.e.
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
A\left(x_{0}\right)=A_{0}+B\left(x_{0}\right)
can take the role of the model operator
A_{0}
.

The second set of conditions concerned with the radial convergence at infinity of
A
and
{\rm d}A
will also only play a role in determining the trace. Note that the prerequisites of Proposition
2.6
and Remark
2.7
are satisfied if
B
satisfies Hypothesis
2.11
.

In the following we will prepare for the proof of Proposition
2.27
. In this proof we will factorize the partial trace of difference of the resolvent powers of
DD^{\ast}
and
D^{\ast}D
into appropriate bounded and Schatten-von Neumann class operators. These factors will fit the templates of operators we will discuss below.

Definition 2.13
.

Let
B
satisfy Hypothesis
2.11
. For
z\in\mathbb{C}
let
w=w\left(z\right):=\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)
. For
C\geq 0
let
\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right):=\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:w\left(z\right)\geq c\right\}
where
c\geq 1
is the minimal choice, such that

\displaystyle Cw^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\rho^{-2N}_{1}\left(B\right)+\rho^{2N}_{1}\left(B\right)+1\right)\left(\rho^{-2N}_{z}\left(B\right)+\rho^{2N}_{z}\left(B\right)\right)\leq\frac{1}{2},\ z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right).

(50)

Definition 2.14
.

For
s\in\mathbb{R}
denote by
Sym_{s}
those smooth functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, such that for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\langle X\rangle^{\left|\gamma\right|-s}\partial^{\gamma}f\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right).

(51)

Remark 2.15
.

Straightforward calculation verifies
C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\subset Sym_{-\infty}:=\bigcap_{j=0}^{\infty}Sym_{-j}
, and
\langle X\rangle^{u}\in Sym_{u}
for
u\in\mathbb{R}
. For
f\in Sym_{r}
,
g\in Sym_{s}
we also have
fg\in Sym_{r+s}
, which follows from the Leibniz rule. Moreover for
f\in Sym_{0}
the Leibniz rule, taking adjoints and complex interpolation implies that
\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{s}f\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-s}
have bounded extensions in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

We discuss our first type of bounded operators involved in the factorization of Proposition
2.27
.

Lemma 2.16
.

There exists a universal constant
C
such that if
B
satisfies Hypothesis
2.11
, and we let
t\in\left[0,2\right]
,
s\in\left[-1,1\right]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq N
, and
r\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
with
r-2\left(k-1\right)\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
, then the operators

\displaystyle R:=\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t},\ T\in\left\{H_{B},DD^{\ast},D^{\ast}D\right\},

(52)

possess extensions in
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
with operator norm bounded by
2^{k}
, for
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
.

Proof.

We write
R=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}R_{i}\right)S
, with

\displaystyle S=\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(k-1\right)+r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}

\displaystyle R_{i}=\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(i-1\right)+r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2i-r},\ i\in\left\{1,\ldots,k-1\right\}.

(53)

We develop
R_{z}\left(T\right)
into a Neumann series around
H_{0}
,

\displaystyle R_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{l}\left(R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)C_{i}\right)^{l}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{l}\left(R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)C_{k}\right)^{l}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right),

(54)

where

\displaystyle C_{i}:=\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(i-1\right)+r}\left(B^{2}+BM_{0}+M_{0}B+\epsilon\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}^{E}B+\langle X\rangle^{-s}\left[\Delta,\langle X\rangle^{s}\right]\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2\left(i-1\right)-r},

(55)

where
\epsilon=-1
for
T=D^{\ast}D
,
\epsilon=0
for
T=H_{B}
, and
\epsilon=1
for
T=DD^{\ast}
. Since
\langle X\rangle^{s}\in Sym_{s}
it follows that
\langle X\rangle^{-s}\left[\Delta,\langle X\rangle^{s}\right]
is a first order differential operator with coefficients in
Sym_{-1}
. Thus there exists a universal constant
C
such that for
u\in\left[-1,1\right]
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\left(-\infty,0\right]
with
w:=\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)\langle X\rangle^{-s}\left[\Delta,\langle X\rangle^{s}\right]}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq Cw^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(56)

Furthermore we have by Hypothesis
2.11
the norm estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)C_{i}}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq Cw^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\left\|\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle_{z}^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(i-1\right)+r-1}\left(B^{2}+BM_{0}+M_{0}B+\epsilon\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2\left(i-1\right)-r}}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cw^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\rho_{1}^{2\left(i-1\right)-r}\left(B\right)+1\right)\rho_{z}^{2\left(i-1\right)-r}\left(B\right)\leq\frac{1}{2},\ z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right),

(57)

where the last inequality follows by Remark
2.5
and the definition of the domain
\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
. This implies that the Neumann series of
R_{i}
and
S
converge in operator norm, each of which can be bounded in operator norm by
2
for
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
. We conclude that
\left\|\overline{R}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq 2^{k}
for
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
.
∎

A similar result holds under weaker conditions, which we will need later in the proof of the approximation statement.

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
B:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow B\left(H\right)
be a point-wise self-adjoint
C^{1}
-function with respect to the strong operator topology such that
\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}=\left\|B\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+\left\|\nabla B\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)^{d}\right)}<\infty
. Assume also that
B\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},1\right)
with
\lim_{w\to\infty}\rho_{z}\left(B\right)=0
where
w:=\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)
. Let
t\in\left[0,2\right]
,
s\in\left[-1,1\right]
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then the operators

\displaystyle R_{k,s,t}:=\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}

(58)

admit extensions in
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
with norm bounded by
C^{k}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2k}
for
w\geq C\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{4}
, where
C
is some universal constant.

For
T_{B}\in\left\{H_{B},D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B},D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right\}
, and
w\geq C\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{4}
, the operators

\displaystyle\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\ s\in\left[-1,1\right],

(59)

admit extensions in
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
with norm bounded by
C^{k}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2k}
for
w\geq C\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{4}
.

Proof.

We note that by Proposition
2.6
the operators
T_{B}\geq 0
are self-adjoint, so that the involved resolvents are well-defined. We show first that

\displaystyle\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(60)

admits a bounded extension. We develop
\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}
into a Neumann series around
H_{0}

\displaystyle\overline{\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(T_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{l}\left(R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T_{\epsilon}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{l}\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(61)

where

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon}:=B^{2}+BM_{0}+M_{0}B+f_{s}\not{\partial}+g_{s}+\mathbbm{i}\epsilon\left(\not{\partial}B\right),\ f_{s}:=2\langle X\rangle^{-s}\not{\partial}\langle X\rangle^{s},\ g_{s}:=\langle X\rangle^{-s}\Delta\langle X\rangle^{s},

(62)

where
\epsilon=-1
for
T_{B}=D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}
,
\epsilon=0
for
T_{B}=H_{B}
, and
\epsilon=1
for
T_{B}=D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}
. Since
\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
is bounded in operator norm by
1
for
w\geq 1
, it remains to investigate the inner factor of the left-hand side of (
2
), and thus we consider

\displaystyle R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}T_{\epsilon}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{2}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}BM_{0}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+M_{0}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}BR_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{s}\not{\partial}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{s}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathbbm{i}\epsilon R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(63)

Since
M_{0}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\not{\partial}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
are uniformly bounded in operator norm by
1
for
w\geq 1
,
B
,
B^{2}
,
\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
are bounded in norm operator norm by
\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2}
, and the functions
f_{s}\in Sym_{-1}
,
g_{s}\in Sym_{-2}
give rise to uniformly bounded operators in operator norm for
s\in\left[-1,1\right]
, the operator
R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}TR_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
is uniformly bounded in operator norm by
c\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2}w^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for some universal constant
c
and
w\geq 1
.
We assume for the rest of the proof that
w\geq c^{2}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{4}
. Then the Neumann series (
2
) converges in operator norm to a bounded operator with norm bound
2
. Since
\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is bounded in operator norm by
1
, we also have that

\displaystyle\overline{\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}

(64)

is a bounded operator with norm less than
2
. In (
64
) we commute
\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}
and obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)^{2}}+\overline{\left[\not{\partial},\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\right]\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}

(65)

By the resolvent identity we rewrite the commutator and obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\left[\not{\partial},\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\right]\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}=-\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left[\not{\partial},T\right]\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\overline{\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\not{\partial}B^{2}\right)+M_{0}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(\not{\partial}B\right)M_{0}+\left(\not{\partial}f_{s}\right)\not{\partial}+\left(\not{\partial}g_{s}\right)\right)\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\overline{\left(H_{0}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}.

(66)

The first and third factor of (
2
) are both bounded in operator norm by
2
according to the previous considerations, the second factor is bounded since
M_{0}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\not{\partial}R_{z}\left(H_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
are uniformly bounded in operator norm by
1
for
w\geq 1
,
\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
, and
\left(\not{\partial}B^{2}\right)
are bounded by
\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2}
, and the functions
\left(\not{\partial}f_{s}\right)\in Sym_{-2}
,
\left(\not{\partial}g_{s}\right)\in Sym_{-3}
give rise to uniformly bounded operators in operator norm for
s\in\left[-1,1\right]
. In total (
2
) implies that
\overline{\left[\not{\partial},\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\right]\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)}
is uniformly bounded in operator norm by
C^{\prime}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2}
for some universal constant
C^{\prime}
. By (
2
) it follows that

\displaystyle\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\left(\mathbbm{i}+\not{\partial}\right)^{2}},

(67)

and thus
\overline{R_{1,s,t}}
is uniformly bounded by
C^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}+1\right)^{2}
for some universal constant
C^{\prime\prime}
, and all
t\in\left[0,2\right]
. Finally, we use that
R_{k,s,t}=R_{1,s,0}^{k-1}R_{1,s,t}
to conclude the statement.
∎

We will also need the following convergence statement, which is a fundamental property of Schatten-von Neumann operators.

Lemma 2.18
.

[
25
]
[Lemma 3.4]
Let
R_{n},S_{n},T_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
be bounded operators in a separable Hilbert space
X
. For
1\leq p<\infty
, let
S_{n}\in S^{p}\left(X\right)
, such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}S_{n}=S
in
S^{p}\left(X\right)
-norm. Furthermore assume that
\lim_{n\to\infty}R_{n}=R
, and
\lim_{n\to\infty}T_{n}^{\ast}=T^{\ast}
strongly. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}R_{n}S_{n}T_{n}=RST,

(68)

in
S^{p}\left(X\right)
-norm.

Inspired by this Lemma and for our convenience, we introduce the following notation.

Definition 2.19
.

If
T
is a bounded operator, and
\left(T_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of bounded operators in a Hilbert space
X
, we say
T_{n}
converges in SOTA of
X
to
T
, if
T_{n}
, and
T_{n}^{\ast}
converge in the strong operator topology to
T
, respectively to
T^{\ast}
as
n\to\infty
.

Lemma 2.20
.

There exists a universal constant
C
such that the following statement holds true. Let
B
satisfy Hypothesis
2.11
and let
\left(B_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of operators, each of which satisfy Hypothesis
2.11
, such that
\left(B_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in the following sense: For
\beta\in\left[-2N+1,2N-1\right]
, and a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have strong operator convergence in
H
of

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\left(B_{n}\left(x\right)+A_{0}\right)^{2}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\left(B\left(x\right)+A_{0}\right)^{2}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta},

(69)

and with
w:=\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)
, assume

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\rho_{z}^{-2N}\left(B_{n}\right)+\rho_{z}^{2N}\left(B_{n}\right)\right)\xrightarrow{w\to\infty}0,

(70)

then we have SOTA convergence in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
of

\displaystyle\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}},

(71)

for
t\in\left[0,2\right]
,
u\in\left[-1,1\right]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq N
, and
r\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
with
r-2\left(k-1\right)\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
.

If additionally for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have strong operator convergence in
H
of

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\partial_{i}B_{n}\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\partial_{i}B\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta},\ i\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\},

(72)

then we also have SOTA convergence in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
of

\displaystyle\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}},

(73)

and

\displaystyle\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\overline{\langle M_{0}\rangle^{r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}},

(74)

for
t\in\left[0,2\right]
,
u\in\left[-1,1\right]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq N
, and
r\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
with
r-2\left(k-1\right)\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
.

Proof.

We only show the statement involving
H_{B}
. The proof for
D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}
and
D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}
is analogous. Consider the factorization presented in the proof of Lemma
2.16
, now with
B_{n}
replacing
B
everywhere. We note that both Neumann series in (
2
), converge for
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B_{n}\right)
. Due to condition (
70
) there exists
w_{0}\geq 1
, such that
\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}:w\left(z\right)\geq w_{0}\right\}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)\cap\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B_{n}\right)
. Thus, analogous to the proof of Lemma
2.16
the operators

\displaystyle S_{n}\left(t\right):=

\displaystyle\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(k-1\right)+r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B_{n}}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t},

\displaystyle S\left(t\right):=

\displaystyle\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(k-1\right)+r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2k-r-t}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t},

(75)

have bounded extensions for
w\left(z\right)\geq w_{0}
, where
w_{0}
is independent of
n
, and their operator norms are uniformly bounded by
2
. By the resolvent identity we find

\displaystyle\overline{S_{n}\left(t\right)}-\overline{S\left(t\right)}=\overline{S_{n}\left(0\right)}\overline{\left(\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2k+r}\left(\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}-\left(B_{n}+M_{0}\right)^{2}\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2\left(k-1\right)-r}\right)}\overline{S\left(t\right)}.

(76)

Since
\overline{S_{n}\left(0\right)}
is uniformly bounded by
2
, it suffices to verify the strong operator convergence in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
of the inner factor of (
76
), to conclude strong operator convergence of
\overline{S_{n}\left(t\right)}
to
\overline{S\left(t\right)}
. Indeed, condition (
69
), the uniform bound given by condition (
70
), and the dominated convergence theorem ensure the strong convergence of the inner factor. For the remaining factors
R_{i,n}:=\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2\left(i-1\right)+r}\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B_{n}}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2i-r}
, we obtain analogous results. For the strong convergence of the adjoints the claimed statement follows similarly
if one replaces (
76
) with

\displaystyle S_{n}\left(t\right)^{\ast}-S\left(t\right)^{\ast}=S_{n}\left(t\right)^{\ast}\overline{\left(\langle M_{0}\rangle^{2\left(k-1\right)-r}\left(\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}-\left(B_{n}+M_{0}\right)^{2}\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-2k+r}\right)}S\left(0\right)^{\ast}.

(77)

using that the operator functions
B_{n}
and
B
are point-wise symmetric.
∎

We also provide here a variant under weaker conditions, which we will later also need.

Lemma 2.21
.

Let
B:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow B\left(H\right)
,
B_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow B\left(H\right)
be point-wise self-adjoint
C^{1}
-functions with respect to the strong operator topology for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and assume that
\left\|B\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}
, and
\left\|B_{n}\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}
are uniformly bounded. Assume also that
B,B_{n}\in C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},A_{0},1\right)
, and that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{w\to\infty}\rho_{z}\left(B\right)=\lim_{w\to\infty}\rho_{z}\left(B_{n}\right)=0,

(78)

where
w:=\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)
. Finally assume that
B_{n}\left(x\right)\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}B\left(x\right)
, and
\nabla B_{n}\left(x\right)\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\nabla B\left(x\right)
for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the strong operator topology in
H
. Then there exist constants
c
and
C
such that for
w:=\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq c
we have convergence in strong operator topology of
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
of

\displaystyle\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}

(79)

for
t\in\left[0,2\right]
,
s\in\left[-1,1\right]
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and the operators are uniformly bounded in operator norm by
C
. In case
t=0
, (
79
) converges in SOTA of
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
. Moreover for
w\geq 1
we have SOTA convergence in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
of

\displaystyle R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)^{k},\ R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{k}.

(80)

Proof.

By the uniform boundedness principle and Lemma
2.17
there exist constants
c,C
such that for
w\geq c
we have the uniform bound

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)},\ \left\|\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{s}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{t}}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq C.

(81)

It suffices to restrict to the case
k=1
by factorizing the operators. Due to the uniform norm bounds and the density of
C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
we may also assume
t=0
. Then with

\displaystyle S_{n}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B_{n}}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}},\ S:=\overline{\langle X\rangle^{-s}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\langle X\rangle^{s}},

(82)

the resolvent identity implies

\displaystyle S_{n}-S=S_{n}\left(\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}-\left(B_{n}+M_{0}\right)^{2}\right)S

(83)

Since the operators
\overline{S_{n}M_{0}}
,
M_{0}S
are uniformly bounded by Lemma
2.17
, it follows that
S_{n}
converges in the strong operator topology to
S
.

For the convergence of the adjoints of (
79
) in case
t=0
, we similarly may restrict to the case
k=1
, and by the resolvent identity we have

\displaystyle S_{n}^{\ast}-S^{\ast}=S_{n}^{\ast}\left(\left(B+M_{0}\right)^{2}-\left(B_{n}+M_{0}\right)^{2}\right)S^{\ast}

(84)

Since
\overline{S_{n}^{\ast}M_{0}}
,
M_{0}S^{\ast}
are uniformly bounded by Lemma
2.17
, (
84
) also converges in strong operator topology.

It remains to investigate the SOTA convergence of (
80
). It suffices to show convergence in strong operator convergence for
k=1
since we may factorize and taking adjoints only changes
z
to
\overline{z}
. The statement then follows as before by the resolvent identity and the uniform norm bounds provided by Lemma
2.17
.
∎

We now present results on the type of operators which we will encounter as Schatten- von Neumann class factors in the proof of Proposition
2.27
.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
B
satisfy Hypothesis
2.11
, let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
,
0\leq\phi\leq 1
,
\supp\phi\subseteq\overline{B_{1}\left(0\right)}
and
\phi\equiv 1
on
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(0\right)}
, and let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy
\left|\beta-\alpha\right|\leq 2N
, and
\left|\beta-1\right|\leq 2N
. Then there exist
\epsilon>0
such that for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}\left(\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\d{B}\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}

(85)

possesses a
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-extension. On the other hand for all
\epsilon,\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}\left(\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta},

(86)

and

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\left(\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}\right)B\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}

(87)

possess
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-extensions. Finally for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta},

(88)

possesses a
S^{d+\delta}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-extension.

Proof.

We first consider (
85
). We note that due to the compact support of
\phi
, for
\epsilon>0
there is a constant
C
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}\left(\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{d}\left(S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right)+\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\partial_{R},d,1+2\epsilon}\left(B\right)\right),

(89)

which is finite for
\epsilon>0
small enough, due to Hypothesis
2.11
. For (
86
) and (
87
) we similarly have for all
\epsilon>0
a constant
C
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}\left(\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{d}\left(S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle+\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\left(\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}\right)B\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{d}\left(S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)}\leq C\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right),

(90)

which is also finite by Hypothesis
2.11
. For (
88
) we have for all
\delta>0
, a constant
C
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta-\alpha}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{L^{d+\delta}\left(S^{d+\delta}\left(H\right)\right)}\leq C\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right),

(91)

which is finite by Hypothesis
2.11
. The claims now follow from Corollary
2.9
, and
\langle X\rangle^{-1-\delta}\in L^{d}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
respectively
\langle X\rangle^{-1}\in L^{d+\delta}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, for all
\delta>0
.
∎

In a similar manner one shows the weaker result under weaker conditions on the operator family
B
below.

Lemma 2.23
.

Let
B:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow B\left(H\right)
be a point-wise self-adjoint
C^{1}
-function with respect to the strong operator topology, and assume that there exists
\eta>0
, such that

\displaystyle\tau^{0,0,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right)+\tau^{0,0,\partial_{R},d,1+\eta}\left(B\right)<\infty.

(92)

Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
,
0\leq\phi\leq 1
,
\supp\phi\subseteq\overline{B_{1}\left(0\right)}
, and
\phi\equiv 1
on
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(0\right)}
. Then there exist
\epsilon>0
such that for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}\left(\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\right)

(93)

possesses a
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-extension. On the other hand for all
\epsilon,\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}\left(\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)\right),

(94)

and

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\left(\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}\right)B\right)

(95)

possess
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-extensions. Finally for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right),

(96)

possesses a
S^{d+\delta}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-extension.

In the next Lemma we prepare the upcoming factorizations of Proposition
2.27
. We will utilize the following notation.

Definition 2.24
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
. For
Y
a Banach space, define the shuffle product
\shuffle
between
l+1
-tuples of operators
T=\left(T_{0},\ldots,T_{l}\right)
in
Y
, and
l
-tuples of operators
S=\left(S_{1},\ldots,S_{l}\right)
in
Y
by

\displaystyle T\shuffle S:=T_{0}S_{1}T_{1}\ldots S_{l}T_{l}.

(97)

For
T
a operator in
Y
,
S=\left(S_{1},\ldots,S_{l}\right)
a
l
-tuple of operators in
Y
, and
k\in\left\{1,\ldots,l+1\right\}
, denote

\displaystyle\iota_{T}^{k}S:=\left(S_{1},\ldots,S_{k-1},T,S_{k},\ldots,S_{l}\right),\ k\leq l,

\displaystyle\iota_{T}^{l+1}S:=\left(S_{1},\ldots,S_{l},T\right).

(98)

Denote for a sequence of operators
\left(S_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, and
\eta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{l}
by
S^{\eta}
the
l
-tuple of operators

\displaystyle S^{\eta}:=\left(S_{\eta_{1}},\ldots,S_{\eta_{1}}\right).

(99)

Finally denote
\mathbbm{1}:=\left(1,\ldots,1\right)\in\mathbb{N}^{l}
.

Lemma 2.25
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
. Let
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\left(-\infty,0\right]
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}}}{{\left|\gamma\right|=N+d}}}\binom{N-1}{\gamma-\mathbbm{1}}^{-1}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes d}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+3}}}{{\left|\gamma\right|=N+d+2}}}\binom{N-1}{\gamma-\mathbbm{1}}^{-1}\left(\left(R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\otimes\left(d+2\right)},R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d+2\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\otimes\left(d+2\right)},R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d+2\right)}\right).

(100)

Proof.

By Hypothesis
2.11
, and Proposition
2.6
, we have for
w=\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)
large enough,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq w^{-\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{1}_{z}\left(B\right)<1.

(101)

We may develop the Neumann series of
R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)
and
R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)
, and obtain

\displaystyle R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)-R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)=2R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\right)^{2k+1},

\displaystyle R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)+R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)=2R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\right)^{2k}.

(102)

So by Lemma
2.1
we have for
w
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)-R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\right)^{d}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\right)^{2k}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right)^{d}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)+R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)\right).

(103)

both sides are holomorphic on
\mathbb{C}\backslash\left[0,\infty\right)
, which implies that the above identity extends to this domain. Differentiating (
2
)
\left(N-1\right)
-times in
z
, yields the claim.
∎

We now arrive at the principal decomposition result. However we will first introduce further abbreviations for the involved operators.

Definition 2.26
.

Let
k,l,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{l+1}
,
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{l+1}
,
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
,
\zeta\in\left\{0,1,2\right\}^{l}
,
\alpha\geq 0
, and
\delta,\xi>0
. For a operator family
B
satisfying Hypothesis
2.11
define the operators

\displaystyle T\left(B\right)^{0}:=

\displaystyle\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right),

(104)

\displaystyle T\left(B\right)^{1}:=

\displaystyle\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\left(\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}\right)B\right),

(105)

\displaystyle T\left(B\right)^{2}:=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)=T\left(B\right)^{0}+T\left(B\right)^{1},

(106)

\displaystyle L:=

\displaystyle\left[\Delta,\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right].

(107)

Now define the operators for
m\in\left\{1,\ldots,\gamma_{k}\right\}
,

\displaystyle u_{z}^{a,b,c,d,k}\left(B\right):=

\displaystyle\langle M_{0}\rangle^{a}\langle X\rangle^{b}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{k}\langle X\rangle^{-b}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{c}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{d},\ a,b,c,d\in\mathbb{R},\ k\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\left(U_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon}\right)_{i}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{i},\epsilon_{i},2\gamma_{i}-1-\delta-\beta_{i},1+\delta,\gamma_{i}}\left(B\right),\ 0\leq i\leq l-1,

\displaystyle\left(U_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon}\right)_{l}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{l},0,0,0,\gamma_{l}}\left(B\right),

\displaystyle\left(V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\epsilon,\zeta}\right)_{i}:=

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1-\delta}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\beta_{i}-\alpha}\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon_{i}}T\left(B\right)^{\zeta_{i}}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\beta_{i}},\ i\in\left\{1,\ldots,l\right\},

\displaystyle\left(W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\right)_{i}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{i},-i\xi,2\gamma_{i}-1-\delta-\beta_{i},1+\delta,\gamma_{i}}\left(B\right),\ 0\leq i<k\leq d,

\displaystyle\left(W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\right)_{i}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{i},-\left(d-i\right)\xi,2\gamma_{i}-1-\delta-\beta_{i},1+\delta,\gamma_{i}}\left(B\right),\ k<i<d,

\displaystyle\left(W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\right)_{d}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{d},0,0,0,\gamma_{d}}\left(B\right),\ k<d,

\displaystyle\left(W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\right)_{k}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{k},k\xi,2m-1-\beta_{k},1,m}\left(B\right)\left(\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1}\langle X\rangle^{k\xi}L\langle X\rangle^{\left(d-k\right)\xi}\right)

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{k}+1-2m,-\left(d-k\right)\xi,2\gamma_{k}-1-\beta_{k}-\delta,1+\delta,\gamma_{k}-m+1}\left(B\right),\ 0\leq k\leq d-1,

\displaystyle\left(W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\gamma,\delta,\xi,d,m}\right)_{d}:=

\displaystyle u_{z}^{\beta_{d},d\xi,2m-1-\beta_{d},1,m}\left(B\right)\left(\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1}\langle X\rangle^{d\xi}L\right)u_{z}^{\beta_{d}+1-2m,0,0,0,\gamma_{d}-m+1}\left(B\right).

(108)

The next Proposition is the central result of this chapter. It is similar to the central result obtained in
[
23
]
, where the statement below was shown without the presence of the partial trace
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
, but with much stronger assumptions on the family
A
, which are not sensible for the calculation of the principal trace. In fact, if we would take the intersection of Hypothesis
2.11
and the assumptions made in
[
23
]
, we would always end up in the trivial case

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)=0.

(109)

Proposition 2.27
.

There exists a universal constant
C
such that the following holds true. Assume Hypothesis
2.11
. Let
\psi,\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, with
0\leq\psi,\phi\leq 1
,
\supp\psi\subseteq\overline{B_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(0\right)}
, and
\psi\equiv 1
on
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{4}}\left(0\right)}
, and
\supp\phi\subseteq\overline{B_{1}\left(0\right)}
, and
\phi\equiv 1
on
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(0\right)}
.
Then there exist, independent of the choices of
B
,
\psi
,
\phi
, and
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
, multi-indices
\beta\in\left[\alpha-2N,\alpha+2N\right]\cap\left[1-2N,-1+2N\right]
,
\epsilon^{j},\eta^{j}\in\left[-1,1\right]^{d}
for
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,5\right\}
, constants
\delta,\xi\in\left[0,1\right]
, and
C_{\gamma}^{j},C_{\gamma,k}<\infty
for
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}
,
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,d\right\}
,
j\in\left\{1,2,3\right\}
, such that
