\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left[\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1},}}{{\left|\gamma\right|=N+d}}}C_{\gamma}^{1}\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\zeta\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{d},}}{{\left|\zeta\right|\leq d-1}}}U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{1}}\left(B\right)\shuffle V^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{1},\zeta}\left(B\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left[\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1},}}{{\left|\gamma\right|=N+d}}}C_{\gamma}^{2}\left(\left(1-\phi\right)U_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{2}}\shuffle V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{2},\mathbbm{1}}\phi\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\phi U_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{3}}\shuffle V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{3},\mathbbm{1}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left[c_{R}\left(1-\phi\right)\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1},}}{{\left|\gamma\right|=N+d}}}\sum_{k=0}^{d}C_{\gamma,k}\sum_{m=1}^{\gamma_{k}}W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\shuffle V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{4},\mathbbm{1}}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left[\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma=\left(\widehat{\gamma},\gamma_{d+2}\right)\in\mathbb{N}^{d+2}\times\mathbb{N}}}{{\left|\gamma\right|=N+d+2}}}C_{\gamma}^{3}\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\widehat{\gamma},0,0}\left(B\right)\shuffle V^{\alpha,\beta,0,0,2\cdot\mathbbm{1}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\cdot\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\left(R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)^{\gamma_{d+2}}+R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{\gamma_{d+2}}\right)\right],\ z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right),

(110)

where each summand in
\left[\cdot\right]
-brackets is a trace-class operator in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
with trace norm bound given by a
z
-independent polynomial in

\displaystyle\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right)+\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\partial_{R},d,1+\chi}\left(B\right),

(111)

for
\beta
running over
z
-independent finite subsets of
\left[\alpha-2N,\alpha+2N\right]\cap\left[1-2N,-1+2N\right]
, and some
z
-independent
\chi>0
small enough, such that (
111
) is finite.

Proof.

We continue with the formula from Lemma
2.25
and further decompose the involved terms. First, we start with the sum indexed over
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}
,
\left|\gamma\right|=N+d
, whose summands, up to factors only dependent on
\gamma
and
N
, are

\displaystyle R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes d}.

(112)

If we expand
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)=T\left(B\right)^{2}=T\left(B\right)^{0}+T\left(B\right)^{1}
in (
112
), we obtain
2^{d}-1
summands, where at least one
T\left(B\right)^{0}
appears, and one summand which only involves
T\left(B\right)^{1}
. The summands which do involve
T\left(B\right)^{0}
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{1}}\left(B\right)\shuffle V^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\epsilon^{1},\zeta}\left(B\right),

(113)

where
\zeta\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{d}
,
\left|\zeta\right|\leq d-1
, and
\beta
,
\delta
,
\epsilon^{1}
, and
\eta^{1}
are chosen as follows. Let
j\in\left\{0,\ldots,d\right\}
such that
\zeta_{j}=0
. For
\delta>0
set
\beta_{0}:=0
,
\beta_{i+1}:=\beta_{i}+\alpha+\delta+1-2\gamma_{i}
. For
\xi>0
set
\epsilon_{i}^{1}:=i\xi
for
0\leq i\leq j-1
, and
\epsilon_{i}^{1}:=-\left(d-i\right)\xi
for
j\leq i\leq d-1
. Set
\eta_{i}^{1}:=-\xi
for
i\neq j
, and
\eta_{j}^{1}:=d\xi
. Chose
\xi>0
small enough such that
\overline{\left(V^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{1},\zeta}\left(B\right)\right)_{i}}\in S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
for all
i
, which exists according to Lemma
2.22
. Lemma
2.16
on the other hand ensures that
\overline{\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{1}}\right)_{i}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, for
0\leq i\leq d-1
, with operator norms bounded by
4^{\gamma_{i}}
for
w
satisfying (
111
). Since
\left(\alpha+1\right)d<2\left(N+d\right)
by Hypothesis
2.11
, we may choose
\delta>0
small enough such that
\beta_{d}<2\gamma_{d}
, which then also implies that
\overline{\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{1}}\right)_{d}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
by Lemma
2.16
.

Next, we decompose

\displaystyle T:=R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(T\left(B\right)^{1}\right)^{\otimes d},

(114)

via
T=\left(1-\phi\right)T\phi+\phi T+\left(1-\phi\right)T\left(1-\phi\right)
. Those summands which do involve a factor
\phi
can be written as

\displaystyle\left(1-\phi\right)\left[U_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{2}}\shuffle V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{2},\mathbbm{1}}\right]\phi,

(115)

and

\displaystyle\phi\left[U_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{3}}\shuffle V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{3},\mathbbm{1}}\right],

(116)

where we set
\epsilon_{i}^{2}:=-i\xi
, and
\epsilon_{d}^{2}:=d\xi
,
\epsilon_{i}^{3}:=-\left(d-i\right)\xi
, and
\epsilon_{0}^{3}:=d\xi
, and
\eta_{i}^{2}:=\eta_{i}^{3}:=-\xi
. If we choose
\delta,\xi>0
sufficiently small, the same reason as above imply that we have
\overline{\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{2}}\right)_{i}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, for
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
\overline{\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{2}}\right)_{d}\phi}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
,
\overline{\phi\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{3}}\right)_{0}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, and
\overline{\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon^{3}}\right)_{i}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
for
1\leq i\leq d
, where the operator norm is bounded by
4^{\gamma_{i}}
. We also have
\overline{\left(V^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{2},\mathbbm{1}}\right)_{i}}
,
\overline{\left(V^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{3},\mathbbm{1}}\right)_{i}}\in S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
.

For the remaining summand
\left(1-\phi\right)T\left(1-\phi\right)
requires more work. First note that

\displaystyle\left[H_{B},\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right]=\left[\Delta,\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right]=L,

(117)

and thus

\displaystyle\left[\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R},R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{n}\right]=\sum_{m=1}^{n}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{m}LR_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{n-m+1}.

(118)

On the other hand
\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}
anti-commutes with
T\left(B\right)^{1}
, since
c_{R}
anti-commutes with
\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}
, because
\partial_{R}
is a component in a orthonormal system given by coordinates.
Consequently, since there is a odd number of factors
T\left(B\right)^{1}
, we obtain, with
\left\{\cdot,\cdot\right\}
denoting the anti-commutator,

\displaystyle\left\{\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R},T\right\}=\sum_{k=0}^{d}\left(-1\right)^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\gamma_{k}}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\left(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{k-1},m,\gamma_{k}-m+1,\gamma_{k+1},\ldots,\gamma_{d}\right)}\shuffle\left(\iota_{L}^{k+1}\left(T\left(B\right)^{1}\right)^{\otimes d}\right).

(119)

Applying
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
, and bringing
c_{R}
around, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(1-\phi\right)R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(T\left(B\right)^{1}\right)^{\otimes d}\left(1-\phi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1-\phi}{2}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}c_{R}\sum_{k=0}^{d}\left(-1\right)^{k}\sum_{m=1}^{\gamma_{k}}R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\left(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{k-1},m,\gamma_{k}-m+1,\gamma_{k+1},\ldots,\gamma_{d}\right)}\shuffle\left(\iota_{L}^{k+1}\left(T\left(B\right)^{1}\right)^{\otimes d}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}c_{R}\left(1-\phi\right)\sum_{k=0}^{d}c_{\gamma,k}\sum_{m=1}^{\gamma_{k}}W_{z}\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\shuffle V\left(B\right)^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta_{4},\mathbbm{1}},

(120)

where we set
\eta^{4}_{i}:=-\xi
. As before
\overline{\left(V^{\alpha,\beta,\delta,\eta^{4},\mathbbm{1}}\right)_{i}}\in S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
, and
\overline{\left(W_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\right)_{i}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
for
i\neq k
, with operator norm bounded by
4^{\gamma_{i}}
. For
i=k
the operator
\left(W_{z}^{\bullet}\left(B\right)\right)_{k}
contains two factors of the type
u_{z}^{\bullet}\left(B\right)
, which both have bounded extensions with operator norm bounded by
4^{m}
, and
4^{\gamma_{k}-m}
respectively, by Lemma
2.16
. The remaining factor of
\left(W_{z}^{\bullet}\left(B\right)\right)_{k}
is

\displaystyle\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1}\langle X\rangle^{k\xi}\left[\Delta,\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right]\langle X\rangle^{\left(d-k\right)\xi}.

(121)

Since
\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\in Sym_{0}
, it follows that
\langle X\rangle^{k\xi}\left[\Delta,\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right]\langle X\rangle^{\left(d-k\right)\xi}
is a first order operator with coefficients in
Sym_{-1+d\xi}\subseteq Sym_{0}
for
\xi>0
small enough. In particular (
121
) has a bounded operator extension with operator norm
C
. Therefore
\overline{\left(W_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\xi,k,m}\right)_{k}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
with operator norm bounded by
4^{\gamma_{k}}C
.

Finally, in the formula of Lemma
2.25
we consider the remaining sum indexed by
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d+3}
,
\left|\gamma\right|=N+d+2
. The summands are up to a constant of the form

\displaystyle\left(R_{z}\left(H_{B}\right)^{\otimes\left(d+2\right)},R_{z}\left(T\right)\right)^{\gamma}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d+2\right)},\ T\in\left\{DD^{\ast},D^{\ast}D\right\},

(122)

which we factorize into

\displaystyle\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\widehat{\gamma},0,0}\left(B\right)\shuffle V^{\alpha,\beta,0,0,2\cdot\mathbbm{1}}\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\langle M_{0}\rangle R_{z}\left(T\right)^{\gamma_{d+2}},

(123)

where
\widehat{\gamma}:=\left(\gamma_{0},\ldots,\gamma_{d+1}\right)
,
\beta_{0}^{2}:=0
, and
\beta_{i+1}^{2}:=\beta_{i}^{2}+\alpha+1-\gamma_{i}
. As noted before we have

\overline{\left(U_{z}^{\alpha,\beta,\widehat{\gamma},0,0}\left(B\right)\right)_{i}}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
with operator norm bounded by
4^{\gamma_{i}}
by Lemma
2.16
. Also
\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}^{E}B\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
by Hypothesis
2.11
, and the fact that for measurable
S
,
\left\|S\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}=\left\|S\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}
. Furthermore also
\langle M_{0}\rangle R_{z}\left(T\right)^{\gamma_{d+2}}
is bounded with operator norm bounded by
4^{\gamma_{d+2}}
by Lemma
2.16
. On the other hand Lemma
2.22
implies that
\overline{V^{\alpha,\beta,0,0,2\cdot\mathbbm{1}}_{i}}\in S^{d+1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
.

We note that by the proof of Lemma
2.22
there exist
z
-independent finite sets

\displaystyle I\subset\left[\alpha-2N,2N+\alpha\right]\cap\left[1-2N,-1+2N\right],

(124)

and
z
-independent constants
C
, and
\chi>0
small enough, such that the
S^{d}
- respectively
S^{d+1}
-norms of the various operators
\overline{V^{\bullet}\left(B\right)}
can be bounded uniformly by

\displaystyle C\sum_{\beta\in I,\chi\in J}\left(\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(B\right)+\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\partial_{R},d,1+\chi}\left(B\right)\right)<\infty.

(125)

∎

To close this chapter, we show that the partial trace of the semi-group difference of
DD^{\ast}
and
D^{\ast}D
is trace-class.

Proposition 2.28
.

Let
B
satisfy Hypothesis
2.11
, and let
t>0
. Then
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}}-e^{-tD_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}}\right)\in S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)
.

Proof.

Let
C>0
be large enough, and let
\rho\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
be small enough. Let
\Gamma:=\Gamma_{-}\circ\Gamma_{+}^{-1}
, where
\Gamma_{\pm}\left(s\right):=-C\pm e^{\mathbbm{i}\rho}s
,
s\in\left[0,\infty\right)
, and
\circ
,
-1
denote the composition of paths, respectively the reversal of a path. Note that
\Gamma
encloses the non-negative real axis once. Then for
\lambda\geq 0
, we have by Cauchy’s integral formula

\displaystyle e^{-t\lambda}=\partial_{\lambda}^{N-1}\left(\left(-t\right)^{1-N}e^{-t\lambda}\right)=\frac{\left(N-1\right)!\left(-t\right)^{1-N}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{e^{-tz}}{\left(z-\lambda\right)^{N}}{\rm d}z.

(126)

Via holomorphic functional calculus we thus have

\displaystyle e^{-tD^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}}-e^{-tD_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}}=\frac{\left(N-1\right)!\left(-t\right)^{1-N}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\int_{\Gamma}e^{-tz}\left(R_{-z}\left(D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}\right)^{N}-R_{-z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)^{N}\right){\rm d}z,

(127)

where the integral exists as a Bochner integral in
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
in the norm topology. Therefore we may interchange
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
and integration to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}}-e^{-tD_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(N-1\right)!\left(-t\right)^{1-N}}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\int_{\Gamma}e^{-tz}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{-z}\left(D_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}\right)^{N}-R_{-z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)^{N}\right){\rm d}z.

(128)

We claim that the right hand side of (
2
) converges even as a Bochner integral in
S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)
. Indeed, by Proposition
2.27
, we have some constant
C^{\prime}
, such that we may estimate the integrand by

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-tz}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{-z}\left(DD^{\ast}\right)^{N}-R_{-z}\left(D^{\ast}D\right)^{N}\right)\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)}\leq C^{\prime}e^{-t\left(-C+\cos\left(\rho\right)\left|s\right|\right)},\ z=\Gamma\left(s\right),

(129)

which is integrable over
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
∎

3
Approximation of the operator function
B

The goal of this chapter is to construct a approximating sequence
B_{\bullet}
of the operator function
B
, which enables us to calculate the principal trace in the following chapters. We remark that a similar but simpler approximation argument has been used as a standard technique for example in
[
32
]
and
[
25
]
with the same goal. In the multidimensional setup in this paper we unfortunately need a more elaborate approximation procedure caused by the requirement that
\nabla B_{\bullet}
should be sufficiently smooth and trace-class valued, a fact which comes for free in the one dimensional case.
Before we proceed with an approximation statement, we present the following convolution result which allows us smoothing of the operator family without destroying its decay properties.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
O(d)
be the orthogonal group in dimension
d
with (bi-invariant) Haar measure
m
, and let
\left(g_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C^{\infty}\left(O(d)\right)
be a delta sequence with supports decreasing to
\left\{\mathbbm{1}\right\}
of
O(d)
with respect to the convolution given by the Haar measure
2
2

2

Since
O(d)
is a smooth Lie group the usual construction of approximate units in
L^{1}(G)
of locally compact groups (cf.
[
20
]
[Chapter 13.2]) can be refined to be smooth.
. For
\psi^{d}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
non-negative with
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi\left(x\right){\rm d}x=1
let
\psi_{n}^{d}\left(x\right):=n^{d}\psi^{d}\left(nx\right)
be the associated delta sequence of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
with
\phi\equiv 1
near
0
. For
\epsilon>0
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:=\left\{f\in C^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{k\times k}\right)\left|\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}<\infty\right.\right\},

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle:=\left\|f\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\langle X\rangle\nabla f\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\langle X\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\partial_{R}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(130)

For
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
define

\displaystyle\delta_{n}\left(f\right)\left(x\right):=

\displaystyle\phi\left(x\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\psi_{n}^{d}\left(x-y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\left(1-\phi\left(x\right)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{O(d)}\psi^{1}_{n}\left(\log r\right)g_{n}\left(Y\right)f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right){\rm d}m\left(Y\right)\frac{{\rm d}r}{r}.

(131)

Lemma 3.2
.

For
\epsilon>0
the operator family
\delta_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
, and
\delta_{n}\left(f\right)
respectively
\nabla\delta_{n}\left(f\right)
converge point-wise to
f
respectively
\nabla f
. For
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
the function
\delta_{n}\left(f\right)
is smooth with bounded derivatives for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let
f_{n}:=\delta_{n}\left(f\right)
. For
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
extend
e_{1}:=\frac{x_{0}}{\left|x_{0}\right|}
to a orthonormal basis
(e_{i})_{i=1}^{d}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For a neighbourhood
U
of
x_{0}
small enough, the vectors
\left(\frac{x}{\left|x\right|},e_{2},\ldots,e_{d}\right)
,
x\in U
, are still a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Denote by
O_{U}\left(x\right):=\operatorname{GS}\left(\frac{x}{\left|x\right|},e_{2},\ldots,e_{d}\right)\in O\left(d\right)
the Gram-Schmidt procedure applied to this basis. The map
O_{U}
is smooth on
U
and thus for
x\in U
, using the symmetry of convolution,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{O_{d}}\psi^{1}_{n}\left(\log r\right)g_{n}\left(Y\right)f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right){\rm d}m\left(Y\right)\frac{{\rm d}r}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{O(d)}\psi^{1}_{n}\left(\log\frac{\left|x\right|}{r}\right)g_{n}\left(O_{U}\left(x\right)Y^{\ast}\right)f\left(rY_{1}\right){\rm d}m\left(Y\right)\frac{{\rm d}r}{r},

(132)

where
Y_{1}
denotes the first column of
Y
. Thus (
3
) is smooth in
x
on
U
. Since
1-\phi
cuts away
0
, we conclude that
f_{n}
is smooth, because all other components of (
3.1
) are clearly smooth. Since
\psi_{n}^{d}
and
\phi
are compactly supported,
1-\phi
is supported away from
0
and
\psi_{n}^{1}
is again compactly supported, one easily concludes by standard estimates on the integrals, that all derivatives of
f_{n}
are bounded. It is also apparent that
f_{n}
, and
\nabla f_{n}
converge point-wise to
f
respectively
\nabla f
, since for each point, due to the compact support of the Dirac sequences, the integrals in (
3.1
) can be changed to take place over compact sets, on which
f
and
\nabla f
are uniformly continuous. It remains to check that there is a constant
C
such that
\left\|f_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}\leq C
. Clearly
\left|f_{n}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\left\|f\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since
\phi
is compactly supported it is also clear that there is a constant
C
only dependent on
\phi
and
s\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle X\rangle^{s}\nabla\left(\phi\cdot\left(\psi^{d}_{n}\ast_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}\leq C\left(\left\|f\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}+\left\|\nabla f\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}\right).

(133)

For the second summand of (
3.1
) note that if a derivative is applied to
1-\phi
we obtain a compactly supported function, hence a similar arbitrary decay result as for the first summand shown above. Differentiation of
f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right)
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{i}}f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right)=r^{-1}\langle\left(\nabla f\right)\left(Y^{\ast}\frac{x}{r}\right),Y_{i}\rangle,\ \partial_{R}f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right)=r^{-1}\left(\partial_{R}f\right)\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right),

(134)

which implies for
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
, and
x\in\supp\left(1-\phi\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle x\rangle\partial_{x^{i}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{O(d)}\psi_{n}^{1}\left(\log r\right)g_{n}\left(Y\right)f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right){\rm d}m\left(Y\right)\frac{{\rm d}r}{r}\right|\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}},

(135)

where
C
only depends on
\phi
. Similarly there are constants
C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}
independent of
f
and
n
, such that for
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}
, and
x\in\supp\left(1-\phi\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle x\rangle^{1+\epsilon}\partial_{R}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{O(d)}\psi_{n}^{1}\left(\log r\right)g_{n}\left(Y\right)f\left(r^{-1}Y^{\ast}x\right){\rm d}m\left(Y\right)\frac{{\rm d}r}{r}\right|\leq C^{\prime}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\psi_{n}^{1}\left(\log r\right)r^{\epsilon}\frac{{\rm d}r}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{1}\left(s\right)e^{n^{-1}\epsilon s}{\rm d}s\leq C^{\prime\prime}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}.

(136)

We conclude that
\left\|f_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}
is uniformly bounded.
∎

We now arrive at the central result of this chapter. The operator family
B
is approximated by a sequence of finite rank valued operator families with improved regularity. In spirit, this statement is close to the approximation argument used for example in
[
32
]
and
[
25
]
in the one-dimensional case.

Proposition and Definition 3.3
.

Denote
P_{n}:=\mathbbm{1}_{\left[-n,n\right]}\left(A_{0}\right)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\left(\phi_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{D}
be an orthonormal basis of
H
, and let
K_{n}
be the finite rank projection onto
\mathrm{span}\left\{\left(\phi_{j}\right)_{j=1}^{n}\right\}
in
H
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Identify the range of
K_{n}
with
\mathbb{C}^{n}
and let
\delta_{l}
be the mollification operator defined in Definition
3.1
with a fixed choice of cut-off functions and delta sequences.

Define for
B
satisfying Hypothesis
2.11
,

\displaystyle B_{n}\left(x\right):=P_{n}B\left(x\right)P_{n},\ B_{n,m}\left(x\right):=K_{m}B_{n}\left(x\right)K_{m},\ B_{n,m,l}:=\delta_{l}\left(B_{n,m}\right),\ n,m,l\in\mathbb{N}.

(137)

Then there are constants
C
,
C_{n}
, and
C_{n,m}
, where
C
is universal,
C_{n}
is independent of
m
and
C_{n,m}
is independent of
l
, such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right),

(138)

for
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right),

(139)

for
\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq C_{n}
, and

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m,l}}D_{B_{n,m,l}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m,l}}D_{B_{n,m,l}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right),

(140)

for
\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq C_{n,m}
, are limits in
S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
.

Proof.

We start with

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B}D_{B}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right).

(141)

We expand both sides of (
141
), according to the decomposition described in Proposition
2.27
, into a finite sum of trace-class operators. Each summand consists (up to a constant scalar factor and possibly a constant bounded operator
\overline{\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-1}\langle X\rangle^{k\xi}L\langle X\rangle^{(d-k)\xi}}
) of a product of operators with factors
u_{z}^{\bullet}\left(B_{n}\right)
,
V^{\bullet}\left(B_{n}\right)
,
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B_{n}\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}
, and
\langle M_{0}\rangle\left(R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}\right)
with upper indices independent of
n
and
B
(suppressed here by
\bullet
), respectively the same factors with
B_{n}
replaced by
B
. We first note that, since
P_{n}
commutes with
A_{0}
and have norm
1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\rho_{z}^{-2N}\left(B_{n}\right)+\rho^{2N}_{z}\left(B_{n}\right)\right)\leq\rho_{z}^{-2N}\left(B\right)+\rho^{2N}_{z}\left(B\right)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\to\infty}0,

(142)

and because
P_{n}
converges strongly to
\mathbbm{1}_{H}
for
n\to\infty
, we also have for any
\beta\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
, and a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, strong operator convergences in
H
of

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\left(B_{n}\left(x\right)+A_{0}\right)^{2}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\left(B\left(x\right)+A_{0}\right)^{2}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta},

(143)

and of

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\partial_{i}B_{n}\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{\beta}\partial_{i}B\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-2-\beta},\ i\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}.

(144)

The prerequisites of Lemma
2.20
are satisfied, such that we have SOTA convergences of of all factors
\overline{u_{z}^{\bullet}\left(B_{n}\right)}
to
\overline{u_{z}^{\bullet}\left(B\right)}
, and of

\displaystyle\langle M_{0}\rangle\left(R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}\right)\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\langle M_{0}\rangle\left(R_{z}\left(D_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B}D_{B}^{\ast}\right)^{k}\right).

(145)

Clearly the same argument also implies SOTA convergence of
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B_{n}\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}
to
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-1}
. It remains to discuss the
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
- respectively
S^{d+1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
-valued operators
\overline{V^{\bullet}\left(B_{n}\right)}
. Since
P_{n}
commutes with
A_{0}
and is constant on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have
V^{\bullet}\left(B_{n}\right)=\widetilde{P}_{n}V^{\bullet}\left(B\right)\widetilde{P}_{n}
with
\widetilde{P}_{n}:=\mathbbm{1}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)}\otimes P_{n}
which converges in SOTA to
\mathbbm{1}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)}
. Lemma
2.18
thus implies that
\overline{V^{\bullet}\left(B_{n}\right)}
converges in
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
respectively
S^{d+1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
to
\overline{V^{\bullet}\left(B\right)}
. We conclude that (
141
) converges in trace-class by Lemma
2.18
for
z\in\mathcal{D}_{C}\left(B\right)
for some universal constant
C
.

For the second step we fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and consider the statement

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right).

(146)

Note that we may not proceed analogously to the first step, since
K_{m}
, in contrast to
P_{n}
, does not necessarily commute with
A_{0}
. However, since
\langle M_{0}\rangle^{s}P_{n}
is a bounded operator for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we note for
B_{n}
the stronger condition below holds.

\displaystyle\tau^{0,0,\nabla,d,1}\left(B_{n}\right)

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\exists\epsilon>0:\ \tau^{0,0,\partial_{R},d,1+\epsilon}\left(B_{n}\right)

\displaystyle<\infty.

(147)

This allows us to simplify the decomposition in Proposition
2.27
to set
\alpha=0
and
\beta=0
in all appearing indices. In particular no factor of the decomposition contains a function of
M_{0}
. We suppress the new choice of indices by denoting them with
\widetilde{\bullet}
. The same decomposition can be deployed for
B_{n,m}
instead of
B_{n}
. Moreover there exists
\eta>0
uniformly in
m
, and a
m
independent constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\tau^{0,0,\nabla,d,1}\left(B_{n,m}\right)+\tau^{0,0,\partial_{R},d,1+\eta}\left(B_{n,m}\right)\leq C<\infty.

(148)

Since
K_{m}
is constant on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have
V^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m}\right)=\widetilde{K_{m}}V^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n}\right)\widetilde{K_{m}}
, where the self-adjoint
\widetilde{K_{m}}:=\mathbbm{1}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)}\otimes K_{m}
converge in strong operator topology to
\mathbbm{1}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)}
. Thus Lemma
2.18
and Lemma
2.23
imply that
V^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m}\right)
converges in
d
th respectively
(d+1)
th Schatten-von Neumann norm to
V^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n}\right)
as
m\to\infty
.
Since
\left\|B_{n,m}\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}
,
\left\|B_{n}\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}
are uniformly bounded in
m
, Lemma
2.21
implies that all factors
\overline{u_{z}^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m}\right)}
converge in strong operator topology to
\overline{u_{z}^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n}\right)}
, and that the last factor of type
\overline{u_{z}^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m}\right)}
in each product of the decomposition converges even in SOTA (because they do not involve
\not{\partial}
). Similarly also
R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n,m}}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}
converges in SOTA to
R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n}}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n}}D_{B_{n}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}
. The domain of convergence for the parameter
z
is uniformly given by
w=\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq C
, for some constant
C
independent of
m
, according to Lemma
2.17
, since
\left\|B_{n,m}\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}
is uniformly bounded in
m
. Clearly also
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B_{n,m}\right)
converges in SOTA to
\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}^{E}B_{n}
. We conclude that (
3
) converges in trace class for
w\geq C
for some
m
independent constant
C
by Lemma
2.18
.

For the third step fix both
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
, and consider the statement

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m,l}}D_{B_{n,m,l}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m,l}}D_{B_{n,m,l}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(R_{z}\left(D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}\right)^{N}-R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}\right)^{N}\right).

(149)

First note that
B_{n,m}\left(x\right)=K_{m}B_{n,m}\left(x\right)K_{m}
, and thus also
B_{n,m,l}\left(x\right)=K_{m}B_{n,m,l}\left(x\right)K_{m}
, and
\nabla B_{n,m,l}\left(x\right)=K_{m}\nabla B_{n,m,l}\left(x\right)K_{m}
. In particular, if we identify
\mathrm{rg}\left(K_{m}\right)\simeq\mathbb{C}^{m}
, there is some
l
independent
\eta>0
such that

\displaystyle\tau^{0,0,\nabla,d,1}\left(B_{n,m,l}\right)+\tau^{0,0,\partial_{R},d,1+\eta}\left(B_{n,m,l}\right)\leq\left\|B_{n,m,l}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}\leq C<\infty.

(150)

Additionally
B_{n,m,l}
, and
\nabla B_{n,m,l}
converge point-wise to
B_{n,m}
, and
\nabla B_{n,m}
respectively by Lemma
3.2
. We use the same decomposition with
\alpha=0
and
\beta=0
from the second step, then Lemma
2.21
implies that all factors
\overline{u_{z}^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m,l}\right)}
converge in strong operator topology to
\overline{u_{z}^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m}\right)}
, and that the last factor of type
\overline{u_{z}^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m,l}\right)}
in each product of the decomposition converges even in SOTA. Similarly also
R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m,l}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n,m,l}}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m,l}}D_{B_{n,m,l}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}
converges in SOTA to
R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n,m}}\right)^{k}+R_{z}\left(D_{B_{n,m}}D_{B_{n,m}}^{\ast}\right)^{k}
. Clearly also
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B_{n,m,l}\right)
converges in SOTA to
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B_{n,m}\right)
. The domain of convergence for the parameter
z
is uniformly given by
\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq C
, for some constant
C
independent of
l
, according to Lemma
2.17
, since
\left\|B_{n,m,l}\right\|_{C^{1}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}\leq\left\|B_{n,m,l}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}
is uniformly bounded in
l
by Lemma
3.2
.
Because
K_{m}
is finite rank, and
\nabla B_{n,m,l}
, respectively
\partial_{R}B_{n,m,l}
can be bounded in
S^{d}
-norm by multiples of
\langle X\rangle^{-1}
respectively of
\langle X\rangle^{-1-\epsilon}
uniformly in
l
, the dominated convergence theorem and Corollary
2.9
imply that the factors
V^{\widetilde{\bullet}}\left(B_{n,m,l}\right)
converge in
S^{d}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
respectively
S^{d+1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
. Together we conclude that (
3
) converges in trace class for
w\geq C
for some
l
independent constant
C
by Lemma
2.18
.
∎

A simple consequence of holomorphic functional calculus and the possibility of choosing uniform integration paths, allows us to conclude the approximation of the principal trace in its exponentiated form.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
B
satisfy Hypothesis
2.11
, and let
t>0
. Then the iterated limits

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD_{B_{n,m,l}}^{\ast}D_{B_{n,m,l}}}-e^{-tD_{B_{n,m,l}}D^{\ast}_{B_{n,m,l}}}\right)=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD_{B}^{\ast}D_{B}}-e^{-tD_{B}D^{\ast}_{B}}\right),

(151)

are limits in
S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)
.

Proof.

According to Proposition and Definition
3.3
, the trace norm bound from Proposition
2.27
holds uniformly in
n\in\mathbb{N}
for
B_{n}
instead of
B
, on the domain
\operatorname{dist}\left(z,\left(-\infty,0\right]\right)\geq C
for
C
independent of
n
. So we may replace
D_{B}
, and
D_{B}^{\ast}
wit
D_{B_{n}}
and
D^{\ast}_{B_{n}}
in equation (
2
) with the same integrable dominant (
129
), and the dominated convergence theorem allows us to pass the limit in trace norm past the integral in (
2
). In a similar fashion we argue for the two iterated limits in
m
respectively
l
, where we might have to modify the integration curve dependent on
n
for the limit in
m
(respectively dependent on
n,m
for the limit in
l
) in the proof of Proposition
2.28
accordingly. This is possible due to statement in Proposition and Definition
3.3
pertaining to the domains of convergence for the parameter
z
.
∎

We present some properties of the approximating sequence
B_{n,m,l}
in the upcoming Remark, which closes this chapter on the approximation scheme.

Definition 3.5
.

For
l,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\in\left[1,\infty\right]
, and a Banach space
X
, denote by
C^{l,p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s},X\right)
the space of
l
-times continuously differentiable functions with derivatives in
L^{p}
over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with values in
X
. For
l,m,n,s\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m\geq n
, denote the Banach space,

\displaystyle C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s}\right):=C^{l,\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}\right),

(152)

with the norm

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{l,m,n}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{s}_{0},\left|\alpha\right|\leq l}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}\right)},\ f\in C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s}\right).

(153)

Let
C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s}\right)
denote the Banach space
C^{l,p}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}\right)
with the norm

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{l,m,n}^{S^{1}}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{s}_{0},\left|\alpha\right|\leq l}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}\right)},\ f\in C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{s}\right).

(154)

Remark 3.6
.

Assume that
B
satisfies Hypothesis
2.11
.

1.

By (
49
) for
\phi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\left|\gamma\right|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R^{\left|\gamma\right|}\left\|\left(\partial^{\gamma}B\left(Ry+x\right)-\partial^{\gamma}B\left(Ry\right)\right)\phi\right\|_{H}

\displaystyle=0.

(155)

Then for a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\left|\gamma\right|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R^{\left|\gamma\right|}\left\|\partial^{\gamma}B_{n,m,l}\left(Ry+x\right)-\partial^{\gamma}B_{n,m,l}\left(Ry\right)\right\|_{\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-i}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{j}}

\displaystyle=0.

(156)

for all
n,m,l\in\mathbb{N}
,
i,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

2.

For all
n,m,l\in\mathbb{N}
,
i,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(B_{n,m,l}+M_{0}\right)^{2}-M_{0}^{2},\ \mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B_{n,m,l}\right)\in C^{\infty,S^{1}}_{i,j}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right).

(157)

The projection
P_{n}
commutes with
A_{0}
and
K_{m}
is finite-rank, which maps into any
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, thus the first statement holds for
B_{n,m}
instead of
B_{n,m,l}
. But then
B_{n,m,l}=\delta_{l}\left(B_{n,m}\right)
, which together with the dominated convergence theorem (amenable since
\left\|B_{n,m}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}<\infty
for some
\epsilon>0
) yields the first statement. The second statement follows again since
P_{n}
commutes with
A_{0}
and
K_{m}
is finite-rank, mapping into any
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and because the range of
\delta_{l}
is smooth by Lemma
3.2
.

4
Construction of auxiliary integral kernels related to the semigroups of
D^{\ast}D
and
DD^{\ast}

The overarching goal of this and the following chapters is to calculate the principal trace. Since we assume Hypothesis
2.11
for
B
, we may facilitate this task, by replacing
B
by
B_{n,m,l}
(and
D_{B}
,
D^{\ast}_{B}
by
D_{B_{n,m,l}}
,
D_{B_{n,m,l}}^{\ast}
accordingly), Corollary
3.4
enables us to then take the iterated limits in the end to obtain the desired result. For convenience of notation, in the bulk of this and the following chapters we drop the indices
n,m,l
, which will be reintroduced at the end.

Let us first introduce some short hand notations.

Definition 4.1
.

Denote by
\Delta_{l}\subset\left[0,1\right]^{l}
the
l
-simplex

\displaystyle\Delta_{l}=\left\{w\in\left[0,1\right]^{l}\left|w_{l}\leq w_{l-1}\leq\ldots\leq w_{1}\right.\right\}.

(158)

We will frequently use the following parametrization of
\Delta_{l}
by
l+1
coordinates:

\displaystyle s_{0}=1-w_{1},\ s_{1}=w_{1}-w_{2},\ldots,s_{l-1}=w_{l-1}-w_{l},\ s_{l}=w_{l},

(159)

which satisfies
s_{j}\in\left[0,1\right]
, and
\sum_{j=0}^{l}s_{j}=1
.
Note that
\mathrm{vol}\left(\Delta_{l}\right)=\frac{1}{l!}
. Denote for a Hilbert space
Y
, a self-adjoint operator
T
in
Y
, bounded below, and
s\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}\right)^{l}

\displaystyle e^{-sT}:=\left(e^{-s_{1}T},\ldots,e^{-s_{l}T}\right)\in B\left(Y\right)^{l},

(160)

and

\displaystyle T^{\otimes l}:=\left(T,\ldots,T\right)\in B\left(Y\right)^{l}.

(161)

Denote by
L^{0}\left(X,B\right)
the measurable functions on a measure space
X
with values in a Banach space
B
. For
M\in L^{0}\left(X,B\right)
denote

\displaystyle M^{\otimes l}:=\left(M,\ldots,M\right)\in\left(L^{0}\left(X,B\right)\right)^{l},

(162)

and for
s\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}\right)^{l}
,
\mathbb{R}^{\geq{0}}\ni t\mapsto N_{t}\in L^{0}\left(X,B\right)
,

\displaystyle N_{s}:=\left(N_{s_{1}},\ldots,N_{s_{l}}\right)\in\left(L^{0}\left(X,B\right)\right)^{l}.

(163)

Define the convolution shuffle product
\shuffle_{\ast}
between
\left(L^{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(Y\right)\right)\right)^{l+1}
, and

\left(L^{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(Y\right)\right)\right)^{l}
with values in
L^{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(Y\right)\right)
by

\displaystyle\left(q\shuffle_{\ast}M\right)\left(x,y\right):=\int_{u\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)^{l}}q_{0}\left(x,u_{1}\right)M_{1}\left(u_{1}\right)q_{1}\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right)\ldots M_{l}\left(u_{l}\right)q_{l}\left(u_{l},y\right){\rm d}u\in B\left(Y\right),

\displaystyle q=\left(q_{0},\ldots,q_{l}\right)\in\left(L^{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(Y\right)\right)\right)^{l+1},\ M=\left(M_{1},\ldots,M_{l}\right)\in\left(L^{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(Y\right)\right)\right)^{l},

(164)

whenever the integral converges absolutely in
B\left(Y\right)
-norm for a.e.
\left(x,y\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

We want to calculate the trace of an operator in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
, and thus it is helpful to find a
B\left(H\right)
-valued integral kernel, and integrate its trace in
H
over the diagonal, which is intuitive, considering the scalar case covered by the well-known theorem by Mercer. A Hilbert space version is provided in the appendix of
[
11
]
, albeit only for
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
d=1
. However for general
d\in\mathbb{N}
the original proof works with the necessary simple changes (which we omit here).

Theorem 4.2

(
[
11
]
)
.

Let
Y
be a separable Hilbert space, and
T\in S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},Y\right)\right)
. Then there exists a unique
S^{1}\left(Y\right)
-valued measurable integral kernel
t
of
T
, such that

\displaystyle\left(Tf\right)\left(x\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}t\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y,\ f\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},Y\right),

(165)

and

\displaystyle h\mapsto\left(x\mapsto t\left(x,x+h\right)\right),

(166)

is a bounded continuous map of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{1}\left(Y\right)\right)
. Moreover

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|t\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{S^{1}\left(Y\right)}{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\left\|T\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},Y\right)\right)},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{Y}\left(t\left(x,x\right)\right){\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},Y\right)}\left(T\right).

(167)

Remark 4.3
.

We note that the variational principle and the separability of
Y
imply that if we find a jointly continuous
B\left(Y\right)
-valued integral kernel
\tilde{t}
of
T
, by the above stated uniqueness, we have already
t=\tilde{t}
as elements of
C_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{1}\left(Y\right)\right)\right)
. Therefore if we want to calculate the trace of
T
, it suffices to integrate the
Y
-trace of the diagonal of the continuous kernel of
T
, if it exists.

The next goal therefore is the construction of a continuous,
B\left(H\right)
-valued integral kernel of
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)
. To that end we will use a classical Volterra series argument.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
T,S
be self-adjoint operators in a Hilbert space
Y
, and assume that
S
is bounded with norm
c
, and
T\geq 0
. Then for
t>0
,

\displaystyle e^{-t\left(T+S\right)}=e^{-tT}+\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{l}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{l}}e^{-sT}\shuffle S^{\otimes l}{\rm d}s,

(168)

where sum and integral converge absolutely in operator norm.

Proof.

Duhamel’s principle implies for
t>0
,

\displaystyle e^{-t\left(T+S\right)}=e^{-tT}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\left(t-s\right)T}Se^{-s\left(T+S\right)}{\rm d}s,

(169)

where the integral converges in operator norm. The iterative implementation of formula (
169
) then implies (
168
). It remains to show that (
168
) converges absolutely in operator norm. We estimate

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{l}}\left\|e^{-sT}\shuffle S^{\otimes l}\right\|_{B\left(Y\right)}{\rm d}s\leq\frac{\left(ct\right)^{l}}{l!},

(170)

which is absolutely summable over
l\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Definition 4.5
.

Let
t>0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle R_{t}^{k}:=\begin{cases}e^{-tH_{B}},&k=0,\\
\int_{s\in t\Delta_{k}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}^{E}B\right)^{\otimes k}{\rm d}s,&k\geq 1.\end{cases}

(171)

Since
\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}^{E}B
is a bounded operator, the integrals defining
R_{t}^{k}
converge absolutely in operator norm. We furthermore note that the norms of
R_{t}^{k}
are absolutely summable over
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
t>0
, then

\displaystyle e^{-tDD^{\ast}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{l}R_{t}^{l},

\displaystyle e^{-tD^{\ast}D}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}R_{t}^{l},

(172)

where the series converges absolutely in operator norm.

Proof.

Apply Lemma
4.4
to
DD^{\ast}=H_{B}+\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
, and
D^{\ast}D=H_{B}-\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
.
∎

Definition 4.7
.

Let
t>0
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
. We define

\displaystyle K_{t}^{-,l}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\frac{l-1}{2}}^{\infty}R^{2j+1}_{t},\text{ $l$ odd},\ K_{t}^{+,l}:=\sum_{j=\frac{l}{2}}^{\infty}R^{2j}_{t},\text{ $l$ even},

\displaystyle k_{t}^{-,l}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\frac{l-3}{2}}R^{2j+1}_{t},\text{ $l$ odd},\ k_{t}^{+,l}:=\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{l-2}{2}}R^{2j}_{t},\text{ $l$ even},

(173)

where the series converge in operator norm.

Lemma
4.6
then allows to re-express the semi-group difference in terms of commutators.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
t>0
, then

\displaystyle e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2}\left(\overline{\left[D,D^{\ast}e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right]}-\overline{\left[D^{\ast},De^{-sD^{\ast}D}\right]}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{-sD^{\ast}D}-e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right)\right]}-\overline{\left[A,A\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{-sD^{\ast}D}-e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right)\right]}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},A\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{-sD^{\ast}D}+e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right)\right]}-\overline{\left[A,\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{-sD^{\ast}D}+e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right)\right]}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}\left(K_{s}^{-,d-2}+k_{s}^{-,d-2}\right)\right]}-\overline{\left[A,A\left(K_{s}^{-,d}+k_{s}^{-,d}\right)\right]}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},A\left(K_{s}^{+,d-1}+k_{s}^{+,d-1}+e^{-sH_{B}}\right)\right]}-\overline{\left[A,\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}\left(K_{s}^{+,d-1}+k_{s}^{+,d-1}+e^{-sH_{B}}\right)\right]}\right){\rm d}s,

(174)

where the integrals converge strongly on
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
.

Proof.

Since
De^{-sD^{\ast}D}\supseteq e^{-sDD^{\ast}}D
, and
D^{\ast}e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\supseteq e^{-sD^{\ast}D}D^{\ast}
,
s>0
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\left(e^{-sD^{\ast}D}-e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right)=DD^{\ast}e^{-sDD^{\ast}}-D^{\ast}De^{-sD^{\ast}D}\supseteq\frac{1}{2}\left(\left[D,D^{\ast}e^{-sDD^{\ast}}\right]-\left[D^{\ast},De^{-sD^{\ast}D}\right]\right),

(175)

which implies the first line. The second line follows by expanding
D=\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+A
,
D^{\ast}=-\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}+A
, and the third line by Lemma
4.6
.
∎

We exploit the fact that
H_{B}
commutes with the Clifford matrices
c^{j}
, and the above expansion reduces if we apply the partial trace
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
t>0
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}K_{s}^{-,d-2}+AK_{s}^{+,d-1}\right]\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(AK_{s}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}K_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]\right){\rm d}s,

(176)

where the right hand side converges in operator norm of
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
.

Proof.

We remind ourselves that
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\langle\cdot e^{i},e^{i}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
, for any orthonormal basis
\left(e^{i}\right)_{i=1}^{r}
, which in particular implies that
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
is continuous from
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
to
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)
, both equipped with the weak operator/the strong operator/the norm topology. We consider the last line of equation (
4.8
) of Lemma
4.8
, and restrict the integrand to
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes X
, where
X=\left(H^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\cap L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{2}\right)\right)
is dense in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
. Since the integral converges strongly on
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
we may interchange
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
with integration. Also we note that all summands in the last line of (
4.8
), which involve
e^{-sH_{B}}
or the operators
k_{s}^{\pm,j}
contain at most
d-1
Clifford matrices
c^{i}
as factors. We consider Definition
4.7
, and otherwise the factors
e^{-rH_{B}}
,
r>0
, and
A
, which commute with all Clifford matrices: We obtain analogously to Lemma
2.1
, that these terms vanish, if
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
is applied. So (
4.9
) holds, at least on
X
. We may estimate for
k\geq d-2\geq 1
, and
R,S\in\left\{\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},A\right\}
, using the fact that
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
is a
C^{1}_{b}
-family with bounded derivatives of finite rank operators with range in
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, and the defining integral of
R_{s}^{k}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{STR_{s}^{k}}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)},\ \left\|\overline{SR_{s}^{k}T}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{c^{k-1}s^{k-1}}{\left(k-1\right)!},

(177)

where
c:=\left\|\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}
, and
C^{\prime}
is a suitable constant. In particular the norms in (
177
) are absolutely summable over
k\geq d-2
, so we have

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{STK_{s}^{j}}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)},\ \left\|\overline{SK_{s}^{j}T}\right\|_{B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{k=j-1}^{\infty}\frac{c^{k}s^{k}}{k!},

(178)

which is
O\left(s^{j-1}\right)
for
s
near
0
, and in particular we see that
\overline{STK_{s}^{j}}
, and
\overline{SK_{s}^{j}T}
are Bochner integrable in operator norm on
s\in\left(0,t\right)
for any
t>0
. Since
e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}
is also bounded, and
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
is a continuous operator, equation (
4.9
) holds by density of
X
in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
, and the right hand side of (
4.9
) converges in operator norm.
∎

We introduce the following building blocks of the desired integral kernel.

Definition 4.10
.

Let
t>0
. Denote for
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle q_{t}\left(x\right):=

\displaystyle\left(4\pi t\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{\left|x\right|^{2}}{4t}},\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ Q_{t}:=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\otimes e^{-tA_{0}^{2}}\otimes q_{t},

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{j}:=

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}Q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}\right)^{\otimes j}{\rm d}s,\ \theta_{t}:=Q_{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{j}\theta_{t}^{j},

\displaystyle r_{t}^{j}:=

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes j}{\rm d}s.

(179)

Remark 4.11
.

In the following we will use the convention, that if a statement is made for
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}
it means that it holds both for
C^{l^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime},n}
respectively
C^{l^{\prime},S^{1}}_{m^{\prime},n}
consistently in place of all
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}
appearing throughout the the statement. If no other domain is specified, it will always be
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

In the following part we will successively construct integral kernels together with estimates, which we build up to arrive at the desired integral kernels needed to calculate the principal trace. The arguments in each step are quite similar, the techniques involved are classical (for example cf. Chapter 2 in
[
6
]
).

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
l,l^{\prime},m,m^{\prime},n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m^{\prime}\geq m\geq n\geq n^{\prime}
, and
l^{\prime}\geq l
. For
t>0
define the linear operators
Q_{t}^{L/R}
, given by

\displaystyle\left(Q_{t}^{L}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y,\ \left(Q_{t}^{R}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f\left(y\right)Q_{t}\left(x,y\right){\rm d}y,\ f\in C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right).

(180)

Then
Q_{t}^{L}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}
, and
Q_{t}^{R}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n^{\prime}}
, and we have a
t
-independent constants
C
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|Q_{t}^{L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq Ct^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+m^{\prime}-m}{2}},\ \left\|Q_{t}^{R}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n^{\prime}},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\right)}\leq Ct^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+n-n^{\prime}}{2}}.

(181)

Proof.

Let
f\in C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
with
\left|\alpha\right|\leq l^{\prime}
. For
\left|\alpha\right|\leq l
, let
\beta=0\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, otherwise let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, such that
\alpha\geq\beta
,
\left|\beta\right|=l^{\prime}-l
and
\left|\alpha-\beta\right|=l
. Then

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m^{\prime}}\partial^{\alpha}\left(Q_{t}^{L}f\right)\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\partial^{\beta}q_{t}\right)\left(x-y\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m^{\prime}-m}e^{-tA_{0}^{2}}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m}\left(\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f\right)\left(y\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m}\partial^{\alpha}\left(Q_{t}^{R}f\right)\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m}\left(\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f\right)\left(y\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n-n^{\prime}}e^{-tA_{0}^{2}}\left(\partial^{\beta}q_{t}\right)\left(x-y\right){\rm d}y.

(182)

There are constants
C_{\beta},c_{r}
, such that
\left\|\partial^{\beta}q_{t}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}\leq C_{\beta}t^{-\frac{\left|\beta\right|}{2}}
, and
\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{r}e^{-tA_{0}^{2}}\right\|_{B\left(H\right)}\leq c_{r}t^{-\frac{r}{2}}
, while

\displaystyle\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m}\left(\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f\right\|_{l,m,n},

\displaystyle\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m}\left(\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{1}\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f\right\|_{l,m,n}^{S^{1}},

(183)

and so the claims follow.
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then there is a constant
c
, such that for
t>0
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(Q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}\right)^{\otimes j}\right)\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}{\rm d}s\leq\frac{\left(ct\right)^{j}}{j!}q_{t}\left(x-y\right),\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(184)

Let
l,l^{\prime},m,m^{\prime},n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m^{\prime}\geq m\geq n\geq n^{\prime}
, and
l^{\prime}\geq l
. For
t>0
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
define the linear operators
\Theta_{t}^{j,L/R}
, given by

\displaystyle\left(\Theta_{t}^{j,L}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{t}^{j}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y,\ \left(\Theta_{t}^{j,R}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f\left(y\right)\theta_{t}^{j}\left(x,y\right){\rm d}y,\ f\in C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right).

(185)

Then
\Theta_{t}^{j,L}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}
, and
\Theta_{t}^{j,R}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n^{\prime}}
, and there is a constant
C
, independent of
j
and
t
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta_{t}^{j,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)},\ \left\|\Theta_{t}^{j,R}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}t^{j-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}.

(186)

Proof.

We begin with the estimate of (
184
). Let
c=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\overline{A\left(x\right)^{2}-A_{0}^{2}}\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}
. We have for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(Q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}\right)^{\otimes j}\right)\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left(q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}c^{\otimes j}\right)\left(x,y\right){\rm d}s=\frac{\left(ct\right)^{j}}{j!}q_{t}\left(x-y\right).

(187)

For the following parts of the proof, we therefore may interchange iterated integration freely. We show only the properties of
\Theta_{t}^{j,L}
, the proof for
\Theta_{t}^{j,R}
is analogous. Let
L_{f}
denote the operator given by
\left(L_{f}g\right)\left(x\right)=\left(fg\right)\left(x\right)
, for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
f\in C^{l^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime},m}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
and
g\in C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, we have, by the Leibniz rule and the ideal property of
S^{1}
, that

\displaystyle\left\|L_{f}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq d^{l^{\prime}}\left\|f\right\|_{C^{l^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime},m}}.

(188)

In particular there is a constant
c^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|L_{\overline{A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}}}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq c^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}.

(189)

We proceed inductively with convention
\theta_{t}^{0}=Q_{t}
. For
j=0
, the statement is Lemma
4.12
. Let
C^{\prime}
be the constant given by (
181
), which is independent of
j
and
t
. For
j\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{j+1}\left(x,y\right)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{s}^{j}\left(x,z\right)\left(A^{2}\left(z\right)-A_{0}^{2}\right)Q_{t-s}\left(y,z\right){\rm d}z\ {\rm d}s,\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(190)

Thus by (
188
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta_{t}^{j+1,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq c^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\left\|\Theta_{s}^{j,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\left\|Q_{t-s}^{L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\right)}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+c^{\prime}d^{l}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\left\|\Theta_{s}^{j,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\left\|Q_{t-s}^{L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\right)}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}C^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}s^{j}\left(t-s\right)^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}{\rm d}s+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}s^{j-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}{\rm d}s\right)

\displaystyle\leq cC^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}t^{j+1-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}\frac{2^{2+\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}}{\max\left(1,j+1-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}\leq\frac{C^{j+1}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j+1}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}t^{j+1-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}},

(191)

which holds if we set
C:=2^{2+\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}c^{\prime}C^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}
, which is independent of
j
and
t
.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then there is a constant
c
, such that for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\|\theta_{t}^{j}\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}\leq e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right),\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(192)

Let
t>0
. Let
l,l^{\prime},m,m^{\prime},n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m^{\prime}\geq m\geq n\geq n^{\prime}
, and
l^{\prime}\geq l
. Define the linear operators
\Theta_{t}^{L/R}
, given by

\displaystyle\left(\Theta_{t}^{L}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y,\ \left(\Theta_{t}^{R}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f\left(y\right)\theta_{t}\left(x,y\right){\rm d}y,\ f\in C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right).

(193)

Then
\Theta_{t}^{L}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}
, and
\Theta_{t}^{R}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n^{\prime}}
, and there is a continuous function
h
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta_{t}^{L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}=t^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+m^{\prime}-m}{2}}h\left(t\right),\ \left\|\Theta_{t}^{R}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n^{\prime}}\right)}=t^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+n-n^{\prime}}{2}}h\left(t\right).

(194)

Proof.

Inequality (
192
) follows from inequality (
184
), and enables us to exchange integration and summation in the following. We have for
f\in C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, by Lemma
4.12
, and Lemma
4.13
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta_{t}^{L}f\right\|_{C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}}=\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{j}\theta_{t}^{j}\left(\cdot,y\right)\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y\right\|_{C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}}=\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(-1\right)^{j}\Theta_{t}^{j,L}f\right\|_{C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+m^{\prime}-m}{2}}+t^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(Ct\right)^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}}=h\left(t\right)t^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+m^{\prime}-m}{2}}\left\|f\right\|_{C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n}},

(195)

for some continuous function
h
.
\Theta_{t}^{R}
can be treated analogously.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
l,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m\geq n
. Then there exists a constant
C
, such that for
t>0
,
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{t}^{j}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq\begin{cases}Ct^{-\frac{l+m-n+d}{2}},&j=0,\\
\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j-\frac{l+d}{2}},&j\geq 1.\end{cases}

(196)

Proof.

We proceed inductively in
j
. For
j=0
, the statement follows from

\displaystyle\left\|Q_{t}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{m-n}e^{-tA_{0}^{2}}\right\|_{B\left(H\right)}\left\|q_{t}\right\|_{C^{l}_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}\leq C^{\prime}t^{-\frac{l+m-n+d}{2}},

(197)

where
C^{\prime}
is some constant independent of
t
. Recall that there is a constant
c
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|L_{\overline{A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}}}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{n,n},C^{l}_{m,n}\right)}\leq cd^{l}.

(198)

Let
R_{f}
denote the operator
\left(R_{f}g\right)\left(x\right):=g\left(x\right)f\left(x\right)
. Then similarly we have

\displaystyle\left\|R_{\overline{A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}}}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{n,n},C^{l}_{m,n}\right)}\leq cd^{l}.

(199)

For
j\geq 0
, we have for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{j+1}\left(x,y\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{s}^{j}\left(x,z\right)\left(A^{2}\left(z\right)-A_{0}^{2}\right)Q_{t-s}\left(y,z\right){\rm d}z\ {\rm d}s,

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\Theta_{s}^{j,L}\left(\left(A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}\right)Q_{t-s}\left(\cdot,y\right)\right)\left(x\right){\rm d}s+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}Q_{t-s}^{R}\left(\theta_{s}^{j}\left(x,\cdot\right)\left(A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}\right)\right)\left(y\right){\rm d}s

(200)

and thus, we have constant
C^{\prime\prime}
, independent of
j
and
t
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\theta^{j+1}_{t}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle cd^{l}C^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}\frac{C^{j}}{j!}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}s^{j}\left(t-s\right)^{-\frac{l+d}{2}}{\rm d}s+cd^{l}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}s^{j-\frac{l+d}{2}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{j+1}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j+1}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j+1-\frac{l+d}{2}},

(201)

if we choose
C
, dependent on
c,C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime},l
, large enough.
∎

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
l,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m\geq n
. Then
\theta_{t}\in C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)
for
t>0
, and there is a continuous function
h
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}=h\left(t\right)t^{-\frac{l+m-n+d}{2}},\ t>0.

(202)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left\|\theta_{t}^{j}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq Ct^{-\frac{l+m-n+d}{2}}+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j}\right)t^{-\frac{l+d}{2}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle h\left(t\right)t^{-\frac{l+m-n+d}{2}}.

(203)

∎

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then there is a constant
c
, such that for
t>0
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes j}\right)\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}}{\rm d}s\leq\frac{\left(ct\right)^{j}}{j!}e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right).

(204)

Let
l,l^{\prime},m,m^{\prime},n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m^{\prime}\geq m\geq n\geq n^{\prime}
, and
l^{\prime}\geq l
. For
t>0
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
define the linear operator
R_{t}^{j,L}
, given by

\displaystyle\left(R_{t}^{j,L}f\right)\left(x\right):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}r_{t}^{j}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y,\ f\in C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right).

(205)

Then
R_{t}^{j,L}
is continuous as a map to
C^{l^{\prime},S^{1}}_{m^{\prime},n}
, and for
0<t\leq t_{0}
there exists a constant
C
, independent of
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
t>0
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|R_{t}^{j,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},S^{1}}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}t^{j-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}.

(206)

Proof.

By (
192
) we have for
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
a constant
c^{\prime}
, such that for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}\leq e^{c^{\prime}t}q_{t}\left(x-y\right).

(207)

We recall that
\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
is finite rank valued and thus, with

\displaystyle c:=\max\left(c^{\prime},\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}}\right),

(208)

we have for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes j}\right)\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left(e^{cs}q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}c^{\otimes j}\right)\left(x,y\right){\rm d}s=\frac{\left(ct\right)^{j}}{j!}e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right),

(209)

and thus we may exchange integration freely in the following. We note that there is a constant
c
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|L_{\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{n,n},C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\right)}+\left\|R_{\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{m,m},C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\right)}\leq cd^{l}.

(210)

With convention
r_{t}^{0}:=\theta_{t}
,
R_{t}^{0,L}:=\Theta_{t}^{L}
, we have by Corollary
4.14
, for
0<t\leq t_{0}
, a constant
C^{\prime}
, independent of
t
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta_{t}^{L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},\left(S^{1}\right)}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq C^{\prime}t^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l+m^{\prime}-m}{2}},

(211)

and thus,

\displaystyle\left\|R_{t}^{j+1,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime},S^{1}}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\leq cd^{l^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}\left\|R_{s}^{j,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l^{\prime},S^{1}}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\left\|\Theta^{L}_{t-s}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{m,n},C^{l^{\prime}}_{m,n}\right)}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+cd^{l}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\left\|R_{s}^{j,L}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{m^{\prime},n},C^{l^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime},n}\right)}\left\|\Theta^{L}_{t-s}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\right)}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq cC^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l-l^{\prime}}{2}\right)}\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}s^{j}\left(t-s\right)^{-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}{\rm d}s+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}s^{j-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}{\rm d}s\right)

\displaystyle\leq cC^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}t^{j+1-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}\frac{2^{2+\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}}{\max\left(1,j+1-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}\leq\frac{C^{j+1}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j+1}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}\right)}t^{j+1-\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}},

(212)

which holds if we set
C:=2^{2+\frac{l^{\prime}-l}{2}}cC^{\prime}d^{l^{\prime}}
, which is independent of
j
and
t
.
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then there is a constant
c
, such that for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left\|r_{t}^{j}\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}}\leq e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right),\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(213)

Let
l,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m\geq n
,
1\geq l
. For
0<t\leq t_{0}
exists a constant
C
, independent of
t
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|r_{t}^{j}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j-\frac{l+d}{2}}.

(214)

Proof.

Let
0<t\leq t_{0}
and
C^{\prime}
be a constant, independent of
t
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\theta_{t}\right\|_{C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq C^{\prime}t^{-\frac{l+m-n+d}{2}}.

(215)

Recall that there is a constant
c
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|L_{\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{n,n},C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\right)}+\left\|R_{\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)}\right\|_{B\left(C^{l}_{m,m},C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\right)}\leq cd^{l}.

(216)

We have for
j\geq 0
with convention
r_{t}^{0}:=\theta_{t}
, and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle r_{t}^{j+1}\left(x,y\right)=\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}R_{s}^{j,L}\left(\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\theta_{t-s}\left(\cdot,y\right)\right)\left(x\right){\rm d}s+\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}\Theta_{t-s}^{R}\left(r_{s}^{j}\left(x,\cdot\right)\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right)\left(y\right){\rm d}s,

(217)

and thus we have a constant
C^{\prime\prime}
independent of
j
and
t
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|r^{j+1}_{t}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle cd^{l}C^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}\frac{C^{j}}{j!}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{2}}s^{j}\left(t-s\right)^{-\frac{l+d}{2}}{\rm d}s+cd^{l}C^{\prime\prime}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}s^{j-\frac{l+d}{2}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C^{j+1}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j+1}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j+1-\frac{l+d}{2}},

(218)

if we choose
C
, dependent on
c,C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime},l
, large enough.
∎

5
Construction of the main integral kernels

With the auxiliary kernels constructed in the previous chapter, we may now proceed to construct the integral kernels of the operators appearing in Lemma
4.9
, which involve commutators.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
t>0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Define

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}^{-,k}:=\sum_{j=\frac{k-1}{2}}^{\infty}r^{2j+1}_{t},\ k\text{ odd, }\kappa_{t}^{+,k}:=\sum_{j=\frac{k}{2}}^{\infty}r^{2j}_{t},\ k\text{ even.}

(219)

Moreover define for
\epsilon,r>0
, with
\not{\partial}_{x/y/1}
denoting the application of
\not{\partial}
to the
x
-variable/
y
-variable/first variable, for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{t}^{\epsilon,r}\left(x,y\right):=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(x\right)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}_{x}+\not{\partial}_{y}\right)\left(\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{t}^{-,d-2}+\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\left(A\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)\right)\left(x,y\right)\right),

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{\epsilon,r}\left(x,y\right):=

\displaystyle-\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(x\right)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(y\right)\left(\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(A\kappa_{t}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]\right)\left(x,y\right),

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{r}\left(x,y\right):=

\displaystyle-\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(x\right)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(y\right)\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(A\kappa_{t}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]\left(x,y\right).

(220)

We collect first some properties of the introduced integral kernels.

Corollary 5.2
.

Let
l,m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
m\geq n
. Let
t>0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\kappa^{\pm,k}_{t}\in C^{l}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)
, and there are continuous functions
h_{k}
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\kappa^{-,k}_{t}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}+\left\|\kappa^{+,k}_{t}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq h_{k}\left(t\right)t^{k-\frac{l+d}{2}}.

(221)

Moreover, for
\epsilon,r>0
, and
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
with
\supp\psi\in B_{r}\left(0\right)
, we have
\rho_{t}^{\epsilon,r},\sigma_{t}^{\epsilon,r},\sigma_{t}^{r}\in C_{0,0}^{0,S^{1}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)
, such that for some continuous functions
h^{1}
,
h^{2}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\rho_{t}^{\epsilon,r}\right\|_{C_{0,0}^{0,S^{1}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq h^{1}\left(t\right)t^{-\frac{1}{2}},\ \left\|\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\sigma_{t}^{\epsilon,r}\right\|_{C_{0,0}^{0,S^{1}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)},\left\|\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\sigma_{t}^{r}\right\|_{C_{0,0}^{0,S^{1}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq h^{2}\left(t\right),

(222)

where the estimate on
\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\sigma_{t}^{\epsilon,r}
holds uniformly in
\epsilon>0
.

Proof.

We have for
k
odd, and
0<t\leq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\kappa^{-,k}_{t}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\frac{k-1}{2}}^{\infty}\left\|r_{t}^{2j+1}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq\left(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j-k}\right)t^{k-\frac{l+d}{2}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle h_{k}\left(t\right)t^{k-\frac{l+d}{2}}.

(223)

For
k\geq 1
even, and
0<t\leq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\kappa^{+,k}_{t}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\frac{k}{2}}^{\infty}\left\|r_{t}^{2j}\right\|_{C^{l,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)}\leq\left(\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}\frac{C^{j}}{\prod_{i=1}^{j}\max\left(1,i-\frac{l+d}{2}\right)}t^{j-k}\right)t^{k-\frac{l+d}{2}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle h_{k}\left(t\right)t^{k-\frac{l+d}{2}}.

(224)

As a first consequence, we obtain the claimed estimate for
\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\sigma^{r}_{t}
, and since
\left\|q_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}=1
, the statement for
\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\sigma^{\epsilon,r}_{t}
also holds, uniformly in
\epsilon
. Letting the involved derivatives fall on
q_{\epsilon}
, which is a Schwartz function, we verified the claimed estimates for
\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\rho^{\epsilon,r}_{t}
, and
\left(\psi\otimes\psi\right)\sigma^{\epsilon,r}_{t}
.
∎

We will also need a more refined limiting result in Schatten-von Neumann class, which we provide now.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
X
be a separable Hilbert space, and denote for
r,\epsilon>0
,
f\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},X\right)
,
T\in B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},X\right)\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left(\chi_{r}f\right)\left(x\right):=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(x\right)f\left(x\right),\ \left(\chi^{L}_{r}T\right)\left(f\right):=\chi_{t}Tf,\ \left(\chi^{R}_{r}T\right)\left(f\right):=T\chi_{t}f,

\displaystyle p_{\epsilon}f\>=

\displaystyle q_{\epsilon}\ast f,\ \left(p_{\epsilon}^{L}T\right)\left(f\right):=p_{\epsilon}Tf,\ \left(p_{\epsilon}^{R}T\right)\left(f\right):=Tp_{\epsilon}f.

(225)

If
T\in S^{p}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},X\right)\right)
for
p\in\left[1,\infty\right)
, then

\displaystyle\chi_{r}^{L}\chi_{r}^{R}p_{\epsilon}^{L}p_{\epsilon}^{R}T\xrightarrow{\epsilon\searrow 0}\chi_{r}^{L}\chi_{r}^{R}T,

(226)

where the convergence above is uniform in
r
in
S^{p}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},X\right)\right)
-norm. In particular the double limit

\displaystyle\lim_{\left(\epsilon,r\right)\to\left(0,\infty\right)}\chi_{r}^{L}\chi_{r}^{R}p_{\epsilon}^{L}p_{\epsilon}^{R}T=T

(227)

exists and equals the iterated limits.

Proof.

Denote
Y=L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},X\right)
. Since
T\in S^{p}\left(Y\right)
can be approximated in
S^{p}
