norm by finite rank operators and since
\left\|\chi_{r}\right\|_{B\left(Y\right)},\left\|p_{\epsilon}\right\|_{B\left(Y\right)}\leq 1
, hold uniformly in
r,\epsilon>0
, we may assume, without loss, that
T
is finite rank. By linearity we may further restrict ourselves to
T=\langle\cdot,\phi\rangle\phi
, for
\phi\in Y
. Since
\left\|\langle\cdot,f\rangle g\right\|_{S^{p}\left(Y\right)}=\left\|f\right\|_{Y}\left\|g\right\|_{Y}
,
f,g\in Y
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\chi_{r}^{L}\chi_{r}^{R}p_{\epsilon}^{L}p_{\epsilon}^{R}T-\chi_{r}^{L}\chi_{r}^{R}T\right\|_{S^{p}\left(Y\right)}=\left\|\langle\cdot,\chi_{r}\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\rangle\chi_{r}\phi+\langle\cdot,\chi_{r}p_{\epsilon}\phi\rangle\chi_{r}\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\right\|_{S^{p}\left(Y\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\chi_{r}\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\right\|_{Y}\left\|\chi_{r}\phi\right\|_{Y}+\left\|\chi_{r}p_{\epsilon}\phi\right\|_{Y}\left\|\chi_{r}\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\right\|_{Y}.

(228)

Since
\left\|\chi_{r}\phi\right\|_{Y}
,
\left\|\chi_{r}p_{\epsilon}\phi\right\|_{Y}
are uniformly bounded by
\left\|\phi\right\|_{Y}
, it remains to show that
\left\|\chi_{r}\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\right\|_{Y}\xrightarrow{\epsilon\searrow 0}0
, uniformly in
r
. However this indeed is the case, since

\displaystyle\sup_{r>0}\left\|\chi_{r}\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\right\|_{Y}\leq\left\|\left(p_{\epsilon}-1\right)\phi\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},X\right)}\xrightarrow{\epsilon\searrow 0}0.

(229)

The remaining statements are a direct consequence of uniform convergence and the interchangeability of limits.
∎

If we apply this approximation argument to Proposition
2.28
and Lemma
4.9
, we obtain the following Corollary.

Corollary 5.4
.

Denote for
\epsilon,r>0
, and
X=H
,
P_{\epsilon}:=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\otimes p_{\epsilon}
, then for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)=\lim_{\left(\epsilon,r\right)\to\left(0,\infty\right)}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\chi_{r}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}P_{\epsilon}K_{s}^{-,d-2}+P_{\epsilon}AK_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)P_{\epsilon}\right]\right)\chi_{r}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\chi_{r}p_{\epsilon}\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(AK_{s}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}K_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]p_{\epsilon}\chi_{r}\right){\rm d}s,

(230)

where the double limit is in
S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)
.

The next Lemma is a table of all relevant integral kernels, and the corresponding operators.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
t>0
,
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, then for
f\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
, and a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta^{j}_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{s\in\Delta_{j}}e^{-sH_{0}}\shuffle\left(A^{2}-M_{0}^{2}\right)^{\otimes j}{\rm d}sf\right)\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\left(e^{-tH_{B}}f\right)\left(x\right),\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}r^{j}_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y=\left(R_{t}^{j}f\right)\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\kappa^{\pm,k}_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\left(K^{\pm,k}_{t}f\right)\left(x\right),

(231)

and for
t,\epsilon,r>0
,
f\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
, and a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{\epsilon,r}_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\left(\chi_{r}\overline{\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}P_{\epsilon}K_{t}^{-,d-2}+P_{\epsilon}AK_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)P_{\epsilon}\right]\right)}\chi_{r}f\right)\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sigma^{\epsilon,r}_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\left(\chi_{r}p_{\epsilon}\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(AK_{s}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}K_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]p_{\epsilon}\chi_{r}f\right)\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sigma^{r}_{t}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\left(\chi_{r}\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(AK_{s}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}K_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]\chi_{r}f\right)\left(x\right).

(232)

Proof.

The pointwise estimates of the involved kernels by multiples of
q_{t}\left(x-y\right)
enable us to use Fubini’s theorem on appearing infinite sums and integrals, and obtain successively the claimed integral kernel properties (
5.5
). For
\sigma_{t}^{\epsilon,r}
and
\sigma_{t}^{r}
in (
5.5
), the claim follows also by Fubini’s theorem, the memberships
\kappa^{\pm,k}_{t}\in C^{1,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)
, and that
\chi_{r}
is compactly supported. Furthermore,
\eta_{t}:=\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{t}^{-,d-2}+\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\left(A\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)
is smooth, because
q_{\epsilon}
is a Schwartz function. We also have for
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)
, via integration by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}_{1}+\not{\partial}_{2}\right)\eta_{t}\right)\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}\left(\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}P_{\epsilon}K_{t}^{-,d-2}+P_{\epsilon}AK_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)P_{\epsilon}\right)f

\displaystyle-\mathbbm{i}\sum_{j=1}^{d}c^{j}\left(\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}P_{\epsilon}K_{t}^{-,d-2}+P_{\epsilon}AK_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)P_{\epsilon}\right)\partial_{j}f

(233)

We apply
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
, change
c^{j}
to the end, and have for
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}_{1}+\not{\partial}_{2}\right)\eta_{t}\right)\left(x,y\right)\right)f\left(y\right){\rm d}y=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}P_{\epsilon}K_{t}^{-,d-2}+P_{\epsilon}AK_{t}^{+,d-1}\right)P_{\epsilon}\right]f\right)\left(x\right).

(234)

Since the right hand side, due to the presence of
P_{\epsilon}
, possesses a bounded extension (which must equal its closure) in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)
, we obtain the claim for
\rho^{\epsilon,r}_{t}
in (
5.5
).
∎

To improve the postulated decay
t\searrow 0
of some of the operators, we show the following formula, similar to Duhamel’s principle.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
T
be a operator in
H
such that
TH_{B}
and
H_{B}T
are densely defined, and such that
\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}
is a bounded operator. Then for
t>0
the commutator
\left[T,e^{-tH_{B}}\right]
admits a bounded operator extension,

\displaystyle\overline{\left[T,e^{-tH_{B}}\right]}=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\left(t-s\right)H_{B}}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}e^{-sH_{B}}{\rm d}s,

(235)

where the integral converges in operator norm.

Proof.

For
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\left[0,\infty\right)
, we have

\displaystyle 0=\left[T,\left(z-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\left(z-H_{B}\right)\right]=\left[T,\left(z-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\right]\left(z-H_{B}\right)-\left(z-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\left[T,H_{B}\right].

(236)

Thus

\displaystyle\overline{\left[T,\left(z-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\right]}=\left(z-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(z-H_{B}\right)^{-1}.

(237)

Let
C>0
, and let
\rho\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
. Let
\Gamma:=\Gamma_{C,\rho}:=\Gamma_{-}\circ\Gamma_{+}^{-1}
, where
\Gamma_{\pm}\left(s\right):=-C\pm e^{\mathbbm{i}\rho}s
,
s\in\left[0,\infty\right)
. Note that
\Gamma
encloses the non-negative real axis once. Let
C^{\prime}>C
and
\rho\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
,
\rho^{\prime}>\rho
, and denote
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma_{C^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}}
. The curve
\Gamma^{\prime}
encloses
\Gamma
once. By holomorphic functional calculus we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\mathbbm{i}\right)^{2}}\int_{\lambda\in\Gamma}\int_{\mu\in\Gamma^{\prime}}\frac{e^{-t\mu}}{\lambda-\mu}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(\mu-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\mu\ {\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\int_{\Gamma}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\lambda\ e^{-tH_{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\left[T,\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\int_{\Gamma}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\lambda\right]}e^{-tH_{B}}=\overline{\left[T,1\right]}e^{-tH_{B}}=0.

(238)

Thus,

\displaystyle\overline{\left[T,e^{-tH_{B}}\right]}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbbm{i}}\int_{\lambda\in\Gamma}e^{-t\lambda}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\mathbbm{i}\right)^{2}}\int_{\lambda\in\Gamma}\int_{\mu\in\Gamma_{\lambda}}\frac{e^{-t\lambda}}{\lambda-\mu}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(\mu-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\mu\ {\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{dicommlemeq1})}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\mathbbm{i}\right)^{2}}\int_{\lambda\in\Gamma}\int_{\mu\in\Gamma_{\lambda}}\frac{e^{-t\lambda}-e^{-t\mu}}{\lambda-\mu}\left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(\mu-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\mu\ {\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\mathbbm{i}\right)^{2}}\int_{\lambda\in\Gamma}\int_{\mu\in\Gamma_{\lambda}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\left(t-s\right)\lambda}e^{-s\mu}{\rm d}s\ \left(\lambda-H_{B}\right)^{-1}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}\left(\mu-H_{B}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}\mu\ {\rm d}\lambda

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\left(t-s\right)H_{B}}\overline{\left[T,H_{B}\right]}e^{-sH_{B}}{\rm d}s.

(239)

∎

The following result follows directly from Corollary
2.9
.

Corollary 5.7
.

For
j\geq d+1
, and
\epsilon>0
, let
F:=\left(f_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{j}\subset L^{d+\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, with
\left\|f_{i}\right\|_{L^{d+\epsilon}}+\left\|f_{i}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq c
,
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,j\right\}
. Then for
t>0
,
s\in t\Delta_{j}
,

\displaystyle e^{-s\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}}\shuffle F\in S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right),

(240)

and there is a constant
C
, independent of
j
and
s
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-s\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}}\shuffle F\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right)}\leq Cc^{j}\prod_{i=1}^{d+1}s_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(241)

For
\epsilon>0
, let
F:=\left(f_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{d}\subset L^{d+\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, and assume there exists
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
, such that
f_{i}\in L^{\frac{d+\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
. Then for
t>0
,
s\in t\Delta_{d}
,

\displaystyle e^{-s\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}}\shuffle F\in S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right),

(242)

and there is a constant
C
, independent of
j
and
s
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-s\Delta_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}}\shuffle F\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right)}\leq C\left(\prod_{k=1,k\neq i}^{d}s_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)s_{i}^{-\frac{1+\epsilon}{2}}.

(243)

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
t>0
. Then for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
n+m\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle x\mapsto\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}A\left(x\right)r_{t}^{d}\left(x,x\right)\in L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right).

(244)

Moreover for any
\delta>0
small enough there exists a function
h
bounded near
0
, such that for
t_{0}>0
,
t_{0}\geq t>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|x\mapsto\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}A\left(x\right)r_{t}^{d}\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}\leq h\left(t\right)t^{-\delta}.

(245)

Proof.

Let
\psi,\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, with
0\leq\psi,\phi\leq 1
,
\supp\psi\subseteq\overline{B_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(0\right)}
, and
\psi\equiv 1
on
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{4}}\left(0\right)}
, and
\supp\phi\subseteq\overline{B_{1}\left(0\right)}
, and
\phi\equiv 1
on
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(0\right)}
. Then, with
w_{t}\left(x\right):=A\left(x\right)r_{t}^{d}\left(x,x\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{t}=-\left(1-\phi\right)c_{R}\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}w_{t}\left(1-\phi\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)w_{t}\phi+\phi w_{t}.

(246)

The last two summands are in
L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)
, since
\phi
has compact support, and
r_{t}^{d}
is continuous. With the estimate from Lemma
4.18
, we then have a constant
C
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left(1-\phi\right)w_{t}\phi+\phi w_{t}\right)\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{1}\left(H\right)\right)}\leq Ct^{\frac{d}{2}}.

(247)

For the first summand
-\left(1-\phi\right)c_{R}\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}w_{t}\left(1-\phi\right)
expand
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)=T_{0}^{B}+T_{1}^{B}
with
T_{0}^{B}=\phi\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}c_{R}\left(\partial_{R}B\right)
, and
T_{1}^{B}=\left(1-\phi\right)\mathbbm{i}\left(\left(\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}\right)B\right)
, in each factor of the definition of
r^{d}_{t}
(see Definition
4.10
). We obtain
2^{d}-1
summands where at least one factor
T_{0}^{B}
appears, and one summand which only involves
T_{1}^{B}
. Let us deal first with the summands with at least one factor
T_{0}^{B}
. We note that
T_{0}^{B}\in L^{d-\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)
for some
\epsilon>0
and all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, owed to the presence of either the cut-off function or the radial derivative. Thus, setting
T^{B}_{\alpha}:=\left(T^{B}_{\alpha_{1}},\ldots,T^{B}_{\alpha_{d}}\right)
, for
\alpha\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{d}
, where for some
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
we have
\alpha_{i}=0
, we have, deploying point-wise estimates on
\theta_{s_{j}}
and on
A\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(A\left(x\right)\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d}}\left(\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}T_{\alpha}^{B}\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s\right)\right\|_{\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}\leq ce^{ct}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d}}\left(q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}F\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s,

(248)

where
F=\left(f_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{d}
is a collection of continuous functions with
F\subset L^{d+\delta}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\cap L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, and
f_{i}\in L^{d-\epsilon}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, for some
\delta>0
small enough. Thus Corollary
5.7
applies, and the right-hand side of (
248
) is the diagonal entry at
x
of the continuous integral kernel of a trace-class operator
S
in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, with trace-norm estimate

\displaystyle\left\|S\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right)}\leq Ce^{ct}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d}}\left(\prod_{j=1,j\neq i}^{d}s_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)s_{i}^{-\frac{1+\delta}{2}}{\rm d}s\leq C^{\prime}e^{ct}t^{-\frac{\delta}{2}}=h\left(t\right)t^{-\frac{\delta}{2}},\ t_{0}\geq t>0,

(249)

for
t_{0}>0
, some constants
C,C^{\prime}
, and some continuous function
h
. Now consider the remaining case
\alpha_{j}=1
for all
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
. By Remark
2.15
, we have that
\left[H_{B},\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right]=\left[\Delta,\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}\right]
is a first order differential operator
P
, with smooth coefficients in
Sym_{-1}
. By Lemma
5.6
, we have for
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\overline{\left[\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R},e^{-\tau H_{B}}\right]}=\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{-\left(\tau-\sigma\right)H_{B}}Pe^{-\sigma H_{B}}{\rm d}\sigma,

(250)

the integral convergent in
B\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)
. For
t>0
, let

\displaystyle V_{t}:=\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle\left(T_{1}^{B}\right)^{\otimes d}{\rm d}s,

(251)

where the integral converges in operator norm. We note that
\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}
anti-commutes with
T_{1}^{B}
, since
1-\psi\equiv 1
on
\supp\left(1-\phi\right)
, and
c_{R}
anti-commutes with
\not{\partial}-c_{R}\partial_{R}
, because
\partial_{R}
is a component in a orthonormal system given by coordinates. Consequently, since there is a odd number of factors
T_{1}^{B}
anti-commuting
\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}
with
V_{t}
we obtain by (
250
),

\displaystyle\left\{\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R},V_{t}\right\}=\sum_{i=0}^{d}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d+1}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{i}{\rm d}s,

(252)

where
C^{i}_{j}:=T^{B}_{1}
,
j\neq i
,
C^{i}_{i}:=P
, and
\left\{\cdot,\cdot\right\}
denotes the anti-commutator. It follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{t}:=-\left(1-\phi\right)c_{R}\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R}V_{t}\left(1-\phi\right)=\left(1-\phi\right)c_{R}V_{t}\left(1-\phi\right)c_{R}+\left\{\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R},V_{t}\right\},

(253)

and by applying
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
and bringing
c_{R}
around, we have

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\widetilde{V}_{t}=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left\{\left(1-\psi\right)c_{R},V_{t}\right\}=\sum_{i=0}^{d}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d+1}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{i}{\rm d}s.

(254)

Now recall that
B
is a operator function which commutes with some finite rank projection
K_{m}
from Proposition and Definition
3.3
, for
m\in\mathbb{N}
large enough, and that
K_{m}B=B=BK_{m}
. Consequently also
K_{m}T^{B}_{1}=T^{B}_{1}=T^{B}_{1}K_{m}
. Furthermore, with
\widetilde{K}_{m}=\mathbbm{1}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\right)}\otimes K_{m}
, we have
\left[H_{B},\widetilde{K}_{m}\right]=B\left[M_{0},\widetilde{K}_{m}\right]+\left[M_{0},\widetilde{K}_{m}\right]B=:K
, where the right hand side is a bounded continuous function with values in the finite rank operators. We have similar by Lemma
5.6
for
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\overline{\left[\widetilde{K}_{m},e^{-\tau H_{B}}\right]}=\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{-\left(\tau-\sigma\right)H_{B}}Ke^{-\sigma H_{B}}{\rm d}\sigma,

(255)

which shows that

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d+1}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{i}{\rm d}s=\int_{s\in\Delta_{d+1}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle\widetilde{C}^{i}{\rm d}s+\int_{s\in\Delta_{d+2}}U_{s}\shuffle F^{i}{\rm d}s,

(256)

where
\widetilde{C}^{i}_{j}=T^{B}_{1}
,
j\neq i
,
\widetilde{C}^{i}_{i}=\widetilde{K}_{m}P
,
U_{s_{j},j}=e^{-s_{j}H_{B}}
,
j\neq i
,
U_{s_{i},i}=\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}e^{-s_{i}H_{B}}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}
, and finally
F^{i}_{j}=T^{B}_{1}
,
j\notin\left\{i,i+1\right\}
,
F^{i}_{i}=\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}K
,
F^{i}_{i+1}=\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}P
, with some
\epsilon>0
. Since the factor
\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}e^{-s_{i}H_{B}}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}
can be regarded as the semi-group generated by
\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}H_{B}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}
, which satisfies the conditions of the Hille-Yosida theorem (cf. the Neumann series argument of (
2
)), we conclude that the semi-group
\overline{\langle X\rangle^{\epsilon}e^{-s_{i}H_{B}}\langle X\rangle^{-\epsilon}}
is uniformly bounded in operator norm for all
s_{i}>0
. If we write

\displaystyle\langle M_{0}\rangle^{1+m}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{i}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{n}=

\displaystyle\langle M_{0}\rangle^{1+m}e^{-s_{-1}H_{B}}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{-\eta}\left(\langle M_{0}\rangle^{\eta}C^{i}_{0}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-\beta_{0}}\right)\left(\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{\beta_{0}}e^{-s_{0}H_{B}}\right)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d}\left(C^{i}_{j}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-\beta_{j}}\right)\left(\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{\beta_{j}}e^{-s_{j}H_{B}}\right)\langle M_{0}\rangle^{n},

(257)

for suitable
\beta_{j}>0
,
\eta>0
, we may estimate the factors with
C^{i}_{j}\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{-\beta_{j}}
in an appropriate Schatten-von Neumann norm by Corollary
2.9
, and
\langle\not{\partial}\rangle^{\beta_{j}}e^{-s_{j}H_{B}}
in operator norm, to obtain a
s
-integrable trace-norm of
\langle M_{0}^{1+m}\rangle e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{i}\langle M_{0}\rangle^{n}
. Careful accounting then leads to the following norm estimate

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d+1}}\left\|\langle M_{0}\rangle e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{i}\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}{\rm d}s\leq Ct^{\frac{d-1-n-m}{2}+\delta},\ t_{0}\geq t>0,

(258)

for some
t_{0},\delta>0
, and a constant
C
. For
\int_{s\in\Delta_{d+2}}U_{s}\shuffle F^{i}{\rm d}s
a similar factorization is possible, and one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{s\in\Delta_{d+2}}\left\|U_{s}\shuffle F^{i}\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)\right)}{\rm d}s\leq Ct^{\frac{d+1-n-m}{2}-\delta},\ t_{0}\geq t>0,

(259)

for some
t_{0},\delta>0
, and a constant
C
. Consequently we have by (
254
),

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\widetilde{V}_{t}\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)\right)}\leq Ct^{\frac{d-1-n-m}{2}+\delta},\ t_{0}\geq t>0,

(260)

for some
t_{0},\delta>0
, and a constant
C
. Now Theorem
4.2
and its Remark
4.3
, imply for the diagonal of its continuous integral kernel, that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(A\left(x\right)\left(1-\phi\left(x\right)\right)\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d}}\left(\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(T_{1}^{B}\right)^{\otimes d}\right)\left(x,x\right)\left(1-\phi\left(x\right)\right){\rm d}s\right)\right\|_{\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{\frac{d-1-n-m}{2}+\delta},\ t_{0}\geq t>0,

(261)

for some
t_{0},\delta>0
, and a constant
C
. Compiling all summands and estimates we see that for any
\delta>0
small enough, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\ A\left(x\right)r^{d}_{t}\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}\leq h\left(t\right)t^{-\delta},\ t_{0}\geq t>0,

(262)

for
t_{0}>0
and some continuous function
h
.
∎

The following Lemma shows what intuitively seems right, namely that the trace of
\sigma_{s}
should vanish, due to the presence of a commutator. We note that at some point in the proof we need that
\left(\Delta B\right)
is bounded, which is the main reason for the introduction of the smoothing procedure in Proposition and Definition
3.3
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
t>0
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\lim_{r\to\infty}\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x\right)=0.

(263)

Proof.

\kappa_{t}^{\pm,k}
is the integral kernel of a self-adjoint operator, and thus

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}^{\pm,k}\left(x,y\right)=\left(\kappa_{t}^{\pm,k}\left(y,x\right)\right)^{\ast},\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(264)

which implies

\displaystyle\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{\pm,k}\left(x,x\right)=\frac{1}{2}\not{\partial}_{x}\kappa_{t}^{\pm,k}\left(x,x\right)=\left.\frac{1}{2}\left(\not{\partial}_{x}+\not{\partial}_{y}\right)\kappa_{t}^{\pm,k}\left(x,y\right)\right|_{y=x},\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(265)

Analogously we have

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}r^{j}_{t}\left(x,x\right)=\left.\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{x}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{y}\right)r^{j}_{t}\left(x,y\right)\right|_{y=x},\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(266)

If we apply
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
and integrate by parts, it follows that
\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{2}\right)r_{t}^{j}
is the continuous
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-valued integral kernel of
\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},R_{t}^{j}\right]}
. We have

\displaystyle\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},R_{t}^{j}\right]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{j}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}T_{s}^{i}\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes j}{\rm d}s+\sum_{i=1}^{j}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{j,i}{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle\left(T^{i}_{s}\right)_{k}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}e^{-s_{k}H_{B}},&k\neq i,\\
\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},e^{-s_{i}H_{B}}\right]},&k=i,\end{cases}\ C^{j,i}_{k}:=\begin{cases}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right),&k\neq i,\\
-\left(\Delta B\right),&k=i.\end{cases}

(267)

By Lemma
5.6
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},e^{-s_{i}H_{B}}\right]}=-\int_{0}^{s_{i}}e^{-\zeta H_{B}}\left(A\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)+\mathbbm{i}\overline{\left(\not{\partial}B\right)A}\right)e^{-\zeta H_{B}}{\rm d}\zeta,

(268)

which implies

\displaystyle\overline{\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},R_{t}^{j}\right]}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j+1}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle S^{j+1,i}{\rm d}s+\sum_{i=1}^{j}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}e^{-sH_{B}}\shuffle C^{j,i}{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle S^{j+1,i}_{k}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right),&k\neq i,\\
-A\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)-\mathbbm{i}\overline{\left(\not{\partial}B\right)A},&k=i.\end{cases}

(269)

Therefore, by the point-wise estimate on
\theta_{t}
, given by Corollary
4.14
, and Fubini’s theorem we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{2}\right)r_{t}^{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j+1}}\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}S^{j+1,i}{\rm d}s+\sum_{i=1}^{j}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}C^{j,i}{\rm d}s,

(270)

which a priori only holds a.e., however, we note that the right hand side of (
270
) is in
C^{0,S^{1}}_{m,n}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)
similar to Lemma
4.17
, with summable norms over
j\in\mathbb{N}
. We take the sum and apply
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
to the diagonal, and so we have for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\left(x,x\right)\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d-1}{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{2j+2}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{2j+2}}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}S^{2j+2,i}\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d+1}{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{2j+1}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{2j+1}}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}C^{2j+1,i}\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s,

(271)

Note that we used that
C^{j,i}
contains
j-1
factors of Clifford matrices
c^{k}
, and thus the second sum starts with
j=\frac{d+1}{2}
instead of
\frac{d-1}{2}
. For
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
we take the norm in
\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}
, and obtain with the pointwise estimate

\left\|\theta_{t}\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}\leq e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right)
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\left(x,x\right)\right)\right\|_{\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}\leq\frac{e^{ct}}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d-1}{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{2j+2}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{2j+2}}\left(q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}f^{2j+2,i}\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{ct}}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d+1}{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{2j+1}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{2j+1}}\left(q_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}g^{2j+1,i}\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s,

(272)

where
f^{j,i}_{k}:=\left\|S^{j,i}_{k}\right\|_{\left(\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{m}\right)^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}}
,
g^{j,i}_{k}:=\left\|C^{j,i}_{k}\right\|_{\left(\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{m}\right)^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}}
, with a uniform constant
c^{\prime}
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|f^{j,i}_{k}\right\|_{L^{d}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}+\left\|f^{j,i}_{k}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}+\left\|g^{j,i}_{k}\right\|_{L^{d}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}+\left\|g^{j,i}_{k}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}\leq c^{\prime},

(273)

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and
i,k\leq j
. The right-hand side of (
5
) are by Corollary
5.7
the diagonal entries of the (continuous) integral kernel of a trace class operator
T
in
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, satisfying the trace norm estimate

\displaystyle\left\|T\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right)}\leq C\frac{e^{ct}}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d-1}{2}}^{\infty}c^{\prime 2j+2}\sum_{i=0}^{2j+2}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{2j+2}}\prod_{l=1}^{d+1}s_{l}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+C\frac{e^{ct}}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d+1}{2}}^{\infty}c^{\prime 2j+1}\sum_{i=1}^{2j+1}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{2j+1}}\prod_{l=1}^{d+1}s_{l}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm d}s\leq C\frac{e^{ct}}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d-1}{2}}^{\infty}c^{\prime 2j+2}\left(2j+3\right)\frac{C^{\prime}}{\left(2j+1-d\right)!}t^{2j+1-d}

\displaystyle+C\frac{e^{ct}}{2}\sum_{j=\frac{d+1}{2}}^{\infty}c^{\prime 2j+1}\left(2j+2\right)\frac{C^{\prime}}{\left(2j-d\right)!}t^{2j-d}=h\left(t\right),

(274)

where
h
is some continuous function. By Theorem
4.2
we thus have

\displaystyle\left\|x\mapsto\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\left(x,x\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}\leq\left\|T\right\|_{S^{1}\left(L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\right)}\leq h\left(t\right).

(275)

An analogous argument shows that

\displaystyle\left\|x\mapsto\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}A\left(x\right)\kappa_{t}^{-,d+2}\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}\leq h\left(t\right),

(276)

for some continuous function
h
. Together with Lemma
5.8
, we have for
n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
n+m\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|x\mapsto\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}A\left(x\right)\kappa_{t}^{-,d}\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}\leq H\left(t\right)t^{-\delta},

(277)

for any
\delta>0
, and some continuous function
H
. In total we thus obtain

\displaystyle\tau_{t}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{1}\kappa_{t}^{+,d-1}\left(x,x\right)+A\left(x\right)\kappa_{t}^{-,d}\left(x,x\right)\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\tau_{t}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}S^{1}\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}

\displaystyle=O\left(t^{-\delta}\right),\ t\searrow 0,\ n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ n+m\leq 1,\ \delta>0.

(278)

Let
\sigma_{t}\left(x\right):=-\overline{\left[A\left(x\right),\tau_{t}\left(x\right)\right]}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
r>0
,
\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}
possesses an integrable dominant in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-norm, by Corollary
5.2
, and converges to
\sigma_{s}^{r}
pointwise in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-norm as
\epsilon\searrow 0
, because
q_{\epsilon}
is a delta sequence. Therefore by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem we have convergence in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
of

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x=\int_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\int_{0}^{t}\ \sigma_{s}^{r}\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\left(x\right)\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}\left(x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x

(279)

Since
A\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},B\left(H\right)\right)
, we have, by (
5
), and dominated convergence, iterated convergence in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}\left(x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x.

(280)

We apply
\mathrm{tr}_{H}
, which we may pull through the integrals by (
5
). Then for
s>0
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{H}\sigma_{s}\left(x\right)=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(A\left(x\right)\tau_{s}\left(x\right)\right)+\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\overline{\tau_{s}\left(x\right)A\left(x\right)}\right)=-\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\langle A_{0}\rangle\tau_{s}\left(x\right)A\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\overline{\tau_{s}\left(x\right)A\left(x\right)}\right)=0.

(281)

Together with (
280
), the claim follows.
∎

We are prepared to conclude this chapter with a trace formula, in which already the theme of an integral over a sphere with radius
r\to\infty
appears. Denote by
S_{r}\left(0\right)
the
\left(d-1\right)
-dimensional sphere of radius
r
around
0
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\langle c,v\rangle:=\sum_{j=1}^{d}c^{j}v_{j}
.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
t>0
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{H}\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)\kappa_{s}^{+,d-1}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\partial_{r}\kappa_{s}^{-,d}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right),

(282)

where the argument of
\mathrm{tr}_{H}
of the right hand side is a limit in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-norm.

Proof.

We start with Corollary
5.4
, and consider the term of the right hand side of (
5.4
) without the limit, i.e.

\displaystyle T_{t}^{\epsilon,r}:=\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\left(\chi_{r}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\left[\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}P_{\epsilon}K_{s}^{-,d-2}+P_{\epsilon}AK_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)P_{\epsilon}\right]\right)\chi_{r}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\chi_{r}p_{\epsilon}\left[A,\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(AK_{s}^{-,d}+\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}K_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\right]p_{\epsilon}\chi_{r}\right){\rm d}s,

(283)

Now Lemma
5.5
, and Corollary
5.2
imply that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\rho_{s}^{\epsilon,r}+\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\right){\rm d}s

(284)

is a
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-valued integral kernel of
T_{t}^{\epsilon,r}
, which is continuous on
B_{r}\left(0\right)\times B_{r}\left(0\right)
. Thus Remark
4.3
implies that the
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-valued integral kernel of
T_{t}^{\epsilon,r}
, which is supported on
B_{r}\left(0\right)\times B_{r}\left(0\right)
, must coincide with (
284
) on its support. Theorem
4.2
also implies that
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)
possesses an integral kernel
k_{t}
, which is normalized by the condition
h\mapsto\left(x\mapsto k_{t}\left(x,x+h\right)\right)\in C_{b}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{1}\left(H\right)\right)\right)
, and by Corollary
5.4
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}k_{t}\left(x,x\right){\rm d}x=\lim_{\left(\epsilon,r\right)\to\left(0,\infty\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\rho_{s}^{\epsilon,r}+\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x,

(285)

where the convergence holds in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-norm, by Theorem
4.2
. We also obtain by Theorem
4.2
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)=\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\lim_{r\to\infty}\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\rho_{s}^{\epsilon,r}+\sigma_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x\right).

(286)

By Lemma
5.9
we thus have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)=\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\lim_{r\to\infty}\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\rho_{s}^{\epsilon,r}\left(x,x\right){\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\lim_{r\to\infty}\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B_{r}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{x}\left(\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d-2}+\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\left(A\kappa_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\right)\left(x,x\right){\rm d}x\ {\rm d}s\right).

(287)

Since
\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d-2}
is the continuous integral kernel of a self-adjoint operator, we have for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left(\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d-2}\right)\left(x,y\right)=\left(\left(\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d-2}\right)\left(y,x\right)\right)^{\ast},

(288)

and thus for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, if we apply the trace
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
, which eliminates the
d-2
summand,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}_{x}\left(\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d-2}\right)\left(x,x\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\Delta_{x}\left(\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d}\right)\left(x,x\right).

(289)

We insert (
289
) into (
5
), apply the divergence theorem, and choose polar coordinates, to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{H}\lim_{r\to\infty}\lim_{\epsilon\searrow 0}\left(r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\left(A\kappa_{s}^{+,d-1}\right)\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\partial_{r}\left(q_{\epsilon}\otimes q_{\epsilon}\right)\ast\kappa_{s}^{-,d}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right).

(290)

Fixing
r>0
, by Corollary
5.2
, the above inner integrands of the right hand side of (
5
) possess an integrable dominant in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-norm, and converge in
S^{1}\left(H\right)
-norm point-wise. So by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{H}\lim_{r\to\infty}\left(r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)\kappa_{s}^{+,d-1}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\partial_{r}\kappa_{s}^{-,d}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right).

(291)

∎

6
The principal trace formula

In this chapter we exploit that we only need the involved integral kernels in their long range limits
\left|x\right|\to\infty
. The first result shows that we do not need to care about which values the operator family
A
has near
0
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For
j\in\mathbb{N}
let
M=\left(M_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{j}
be measurable families of bounded operators in
\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}
, with

\displaystyle\left\|M_{i}\left(x\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}\leq C,

(292)

for some constant
C<\infty
. Assume also that there are measurable functions

\displaystyle\left(0,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni\left(t,x,y\right)\mapsto k_{t}^{i}\left(x,y\right)\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes B\left(H\right),\ i\in\left\{0,\ldots,j\right\},

(293)

satisfying for some constant
c<\infty
,

\displaystyle\left\|k_{t}^{i}\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}\leq e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right),\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ t>0.

(294)

Denote for
R>0
,
M^{\prime}:=\left(M_{i}^{\prime}\right)_{i=1}^{j}
, with
M_{i}^{\prime}\in\left\{M_{i},M_{i}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)^{c}}\right\}
,
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,j\right\}
with at least one
i_{0}\in\left\{1,\ldots,j\right\}
, such that
M_{i_{0}}^{\prime}=M_{i_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)^{c}}
. Then there is a constant
c^{\prime}
independent of
j
, such that for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}M-k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}M^{\prime}\right)\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}{\rm d}s\ {\rm d}S_{R}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}e^{ct}\frac{\left(2Ct\right)^{j}}{j!}R^{-1}.

(295)

Proof.

Since
M
, and
M^{\prime}
are uniformly bounded in
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}
-norm by some constant
C
, we have for
t>0
,
x\in S_{R}\left(0\right)
,

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}M-k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}M^{\prime}\right)\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{ct}C^{j}\left(2^{j}-1\right)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{u^{j-1}}{\left(j-1\right)!}\int_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)}q_{t-u}\left(x-y\right)q_{u}\left(y-x\right){\rm d}y\ {\rm d}u

(296)

Since
\mathrm{dist}\left(x,y\right)\geq\frac{R}{2}
, there exist constants
c_{d},c_{d}^{\prime}
only dependent on
d
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)}q_{t-u}\left(x-y\right)q_{u}\left(y-x\right){\rm d}u\leq R^{d}c_{d}\left(\sup_{\sigma>0}\sigma^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{R^{2}}{16\sigma}}\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda=\frac{R^{2}}{\sigma}}}{{=}}R^{d}c_{d}\left(R^{-d}\sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda^{\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{\lambda}{16}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{d}^{\prime}R^{-d}.

(297)

Compiling (
6
) and (
6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}M-k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(M\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)^{c}}\right)\right)\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{ct}c^{\prime}_{d}C^{j}\left(2^{j}-1\right)\frac{t^{j}}{j!}R^{-d}.

(298)

Integration over
S_{R}\left(0\right)
yields the claim.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
j\geq d
, and let
F:=\left(F_{k}\right)_{k=1}^{j}
be a tuple of measurable
B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)
-valued functions, which satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\langle X\rangle F_{k}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}\leq C<\infty.

(299)

Let there be measurable functions

\displaystyle\left(0,\infty\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni\left(t,x,y\right)\mapsto k_{t}^{i}\left(x,y\right)\in B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right),\ i\in\left\{0,\ldots,j\right\},

(300)

satisfying for some constant
c<\infty
,

\displaystyle\left\|k_{t}^{i}\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{n}\right)}\leq e^{ct}q_{t}\left(x-y\right),\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ t>0.

(301)

Then, for
t_{0}>0
there is a constant
C^{\prime}
independent of
j
, such that for
0<t\leq t_{0}
,
R>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}F\right)\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}{\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x\leq C^{\prime}e^{ct}\frac{\left(2C\right)^{j}}{j!}t^{j-\frac{d}{2}}R^{-1}.

(302)

Proof.

Let
R>0
and consider first that all
F_{k}
are supported outside of
B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)
. Then

\displaystyle R\left\|F_{k}\left(x\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}\leq 2C,

(303)

for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. So

\displaystyle\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{j}}\left\|\left(k_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}F\right)\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}{\rm d}s\ {\rm d}x\leq e^{ct}\left(4\pi t\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\frac{\left(2Ct\right)^{j}}{j!}\mathrm{vol}S_{R}\left(0\right)R^{-j}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}e^{ct}\frac{\left(2C\right)^{j}}{j!}t^{j-\frac{d}{2}}R^{-1}.

(304)

The general case follows by Lemma
6.1
.
∎

The next lemma shows that we may delete all higher summands
r^{j}_{t}
,
j\geq d
, which make up the kernels
\kappa^{+,d-1}
and
\kappa^{-,d}
. In particular, the term in the trace formula of Proposition
5.10
which involves the radial derivative is also eliminated.

Lemma 6.3
.

For
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)r_{s}^{d-1}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right).

(305)

Proof.

Let
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, and denote by
\nabla^{y}
the directional derivative along
y
. We have for
r>0
,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\kappa_{s}^{-,d}\left(ry,ry\right)=\sum_{j=\frac{d-1}{2}}^{\infty}\left(\left(\nabla_{1}^{y}+\nabla_{2}^{y}\right)r_{s}^{2j+1}\right)\left(ry,ry\right),

(306)

where the sum converges as continuous functions uniformly in
r,y
. By integration by parts,
\left(\nabla_{1}^{y}+\nabla_{2}^{y}\right)r_{s}^{j}
is the continuous integral kernel of the operator
\overline{\left[\nabla^{y},R_{s}^{j}\right]}
, and so, by Lemma
5.6
, and similar to the proof of Lemma
5.9
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{1}^{y}+\nabla_{2}^{y}\right)r_{s}^{j}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{j+1}\int_{u\in s\Delta_{j+1}}\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}S^{j+1,i}{\rm d}s+\sum_{i=1}^{j}\int_{u\in s\Delta_{j}}\theta_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}T^{j,i}{\rm d}s,

\displaystyle S^{j+1,i}_{k}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right),&k\neq i,\\
-A\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)-\mathbbm{i}\overline{\left(\not{\partial}B\right)A},&k=i,\end{cases}\ T^{j,i}_{k}:=\begin{cases}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right),&k\neq i,\\
-\mathbbm{i}\left(\nabla^{y}\not{\partial}B\right),&k=i.\end{cases}

(307)

In particular, for
n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
C
, such that for all
i,j,k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\langle X\rangle S_{k}^{j+1,i}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}+\left\|\langle X\rangle T_{k}^{j,i}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C<\infty.

(308)

Lemma
6.2
implies that for
t>0
,

\displaystyle R^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\left\|\partial_{r}\kappa_{s}^{-,d}\left(ry,ry\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)}{\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}R^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}e^{cs}\sum_{j=\frac{d-1}{2}}^{\infty}\left(\left(2j+2\right)\frac{\left(2C\right)^{2j+2}}{\left(2j+2\right)!}s^{2j+2-\frac{d}{2}}+\left(2j+1\right)\frac{\left(2C\right)^{2j+1}}{\left(2j+1\right)!}s^{2j+1-\frac{d}{2}}\right){\rm d}s\xrightarrow{R\to\infty}0.

(309)

Similarly, since
A\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}
is uniformly bounded, say by
c^{\prime}
, we have by Lemma
6.2
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\left\|A\left(x\right)\kappa_{s}^{+,d+1}\left(x,x\right)\right\|_{B\left(\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right)}{\rm d}S_{R}\left(x\right)\ {\rm d}s\leq c^{\prime}C^{\prime}R^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}e^{cs}\sum_{j=\frac{d+1}{2}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(2C\right)^{2j}}{\left(2j\right)!}s^{2j-\frac{d}{2}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{R\to\infty}0.

(310)

The claim then follows from (
6
), (
6
), and Proposition
5.10
.
∎

We are now ready to simplify the right hand side of the principal trace formula to its penultimate form, which has its own raison d’être, in so far as it exhibits clearly a similar form as the classical Callias index formula (i.e. a limit of a
d-1
form integrated over spheres with radii tending to infinity). However it is not the final form, because we should not forget that
A=A_{n,m,l}
is still an approximant. Our general conditions on the family
A
do not suffice to enable us to go to the limit
n,m,l\to\infty
in the trace formula of the lemma below.

Lemma 6.4
.

For
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}

\displaystyle\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}_{H}

\displaystyle\left(\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\left(4\pi s\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}A\left(ry\right)\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d-1}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{\alpha\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}^{d-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.y^{j}\epsilon_{\left(j,\alpha\right)}e^{-uA\left(ry\right)^{2}}\shuffle\left(\left(\partial_{\alpha_{1}}^{E}A\right)\left(ry\right),\ldots,\left(\partial_{\alpha_{d-1}}^{E}A\right)\left(ry\right)\right){\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s\right),

(311)

where
\epsilon_{\left(j,\alpha\right)}
denotes the Levi-Civita symbol of the tuple
\left(j,\alpha\right)\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}^{d}
.

Proof.

For
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, denote
\left(M_{x_{0}}f\right)\left(x\right):=A\left(x_{0}\right)f\left(x\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
f\in\mathrm{Dom}\ M_{0}
, and
H_{x_{0}}:=\Delta+M_{x_{0}}^{2}
, which is self-adjoint on
\mathrm{Dom}\ H_{0}
. The smooth integral kernel
Q^{x_{0}}_{t}
of
e^{-tH_{x_{0}}}
,
t>0
, is given by

\displaystyle Q^{x_{0}}_{t}\left(x,y\right)=q_{t}\left(x-y\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\otimes e^{-tA\left(x_{0}\right)^{2}},\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(312)

Duhamel’s principle yields
e^{-tH_{B}}=e^{-tH_{x_{0}}}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\left(t-s\right)H_{x_{0}}}\left(A^{2}-M_{x_{0}}^{2}\right)e^{-sH_{B}}{\rm d}s
, which implies for a.e.
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\theta_{t}\left(x,y\right)=Q^{x_{0}}_{t}\left(x,y\right)-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q^{x_{0}}_{t-s}\left(x,z\right)\left(A\left(z\right)^{2}-A\left(x_{0}\right)^{2}\right)\theta_{s}\left(z,y\right){\rm d}z\ {\rm d}s.

(313)

In fact the right hand side is an element of
C_{m,n}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2d}\right)
,
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, which can be shown by using Lemma
4.12
, Corollary
4.14
, and Corollary
4.16
in a similar way as (
4
), and (
4
). So (
313
) holds everywhere. We find,

\displaystyle r_{t}^{d-1}\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right)=

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d-1}}\left(\left(Q_{s_{0}}^{x_{0}},\theta_{s_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{s_{d-1}}\right)\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d}}\left(\left(Q_{s_{0}}^{x_{0}},\theta_{s_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{s_{d}}\right)\shuffle_{\ast}\left(A^{2}-A\left(x_{0}\right)^{2},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right){\rm d}s.

(314)

By Lemma
6.1
, we have for
n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
A\left(x\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}\leq C^{\prime}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
B_{x_{0}}:=A^{2}-A\left(x_{0}\right)^{2}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d}}

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(x_{0}\right)\left(\left(Q_{u_{0}}^{x_{0}},\theta_{\left(u_{1},\ldots,u_{d}\right)}\right)\shuffle_{\ast}\left(B_{x_{0}},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}

\displaystyle{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{R}\left(x_{0}\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d}}

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(x_{0}\right)\left(\left(Q_{u_{0}}^{x_{0}},\theta_{\left(u_{1},\ldots,u_{d}\right)}\right)\shuffle_{\ast}\left(B_{x_{0}},\left(\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)^{c}}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-n}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}}

\displaystyle{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{R}\left(x_{0}\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}C^{d-1}\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{cs}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{s-u}\left(Ry-z\right)\left\|A\left(z\right)^{2}-A\left(Ry\right)^{2}\right\|_{B\left(H,\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}\right)}

\displaystyle q_{u}\left(z-Ry\right){\rm d}z\ \frac{u^{d-1}}{\left(d-1\right)!}{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}C^{d-1}\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{cs}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{s-u}\left(w\right)q_{u}\left(w\right)\left\|A\left(Ry+w\right)^{2}-A\left(Ry\right)^{2}\right\|_{B\left(H,\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}\right)}

\displaystyle{\rm d}w\ \frac{u^{d-1}}{\left(d-1\right)!}{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s.

(315)

The integrand of the last line of (
6
) has an integrable dominant, since

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(Ry+w\right)^{2}-A\left(Ry\right)^{2}\right\|_{B\left(H,\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}\right)},

(316)

is uniformly bounded (since
B
is uniformly bounded with values in
\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-m}B\left(H\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{n}
for any
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
), and we may take the limit
R\to\infty
inside the integrals, which yields
0
. Therefore

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)r_{s}^{d-1}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left(\left(Q_{u_{0}}^{ry},\theta_{u_{1},\ldots,u_{d-1}}\right)\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\left(ry,ry\right){\rm d}u\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

(317)

By iterating the procedure, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)r_{s}^{d-1}\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)p_{s}\left(ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s,

(318)

where for
t>0
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we define,

\displaystyle p_{t}\left(x_{0}\right):=\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d-1}}\left(Q^{x_{0}}_{s}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right){\rm d}s.

(319)

Step 2:
Denote
\left(N_{x_{0}}f\right)\left(x\right):=\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)\left(x_{0}\right)f\left(x\right)
,
f\in\mathrm{Dom}\ M_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d-1}}\left\|A\left(x_{0}\right)Q_{u}^{x_{0}}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)-N_{x_{0}},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-2\right)}\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes B\left(H\right)}

\displaystyle{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{R}\left(x_{0}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d-1}}

\displaystyle\left\|A\left(x_{0}\right)\left(Q_{u}^{x_{0}}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)-N_{x_{0}},\left(\mathbbm{1}_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\left(0\right)^{c}}\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\right)^{\otimes\left(d-2\right)}\right)\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}\otimes B\left(H\right)}{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{R}\left(x_{0}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}C^{d-1}\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{cs}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{s-u}\left(Ry-z\right)R\left\|\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\left(z\right)-\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\left(Ry\right)\right\|_{B\left(H,\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}\right)}

\displaystyle q_{u}\left(z-Ry\right){\rm d}z\ \frac{u^{d-2}}{\left(d-2\right)!}{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}C^{d-1}\limsup_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}e^{cs}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}R\left\|\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\left(Ry+w\right)-\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\left(Ry\right)\right\|_{B\left(H,\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}\right)}

\displaystyle q_{s-u}\left(w\right)q_{u}\left(w\right){\rm d}w\ \frac{u^{d-2}}{\left(d-2\right)!}{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s.

(320)

By the same reasoning as for (
6
), we may take the limit
R\to\infty
inside the integrals in (
6
), which is
0
. Thus,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)p_{s}\left(ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\int_{u\in s\Delta_{d-1}}\left(Q_{u}^{ry}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(N_{ry},\left(\mathbbm{i}\not{\partial}B\right)^{\otimes\left(d-2\right)}\right)\right)\left(ry,ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s.

(321)

By iterating the procedure, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)p_{s}\left(ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to\infty}r^{d-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{S_{1}\left(0\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle A\left(ry\right)\pi_{s}\left(ry\right)\right){\rm d}S_{1}\left(y\right)\ {\rm d}s,

(322)

where for
t>0
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\pi_{t}\left(x_{0}\right):=\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d-1}}\left(Q_{s}^{x_{0}}\shuffle_{\ast}\left(N_{x_{0}}\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}\right)\left(x_{0},x_{0}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(4\pi t\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in t\Delta_{d-1}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}^{\otimes d}\otimes e^{-sA\left(x_{0}\right)^{2}}\right)\shuffle\left(\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)\left(x_{0}\right)\right)^{\otimes\left(d-1\right)}{\rm d}s,

(323)

The claim then follows if we apply
\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}
to
\mathbbm{i}\langle c,y\rangle\pi_{s}
, using Lemma
2.1
.
∎

6.1
The main result

Throughout the previous chapters, we used tacitly the approximation family
B_{n,m,l}
instead of
B
for notational brevity. Now we are prepared to take the limits
n\to\infty
,
m\to\infty
,
l\to\infty
to arrive at the principal trace formula, which is our main result, presented in the following Theorem.

If we assume Hypothesis
2.11
, by Remark
2.12
, the model operator
A_{0}
is allowed to be
A\left(x_{0}\right)
, for a.e.
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We fix such a
x_{0}
, and set
A_{0}=A\left(x_{0}\right)
in the following.

Theorem 6.5
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
. For
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
denote
A_{\phi}:=A_{0}+\left(1-\phi\right)\left(A-A_{0}\right)
. Then for
t>0
, and any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi}\right)e^{-ts_{j}A_{\phi}^{2}}\right){\rm d}s,

(324)

where
\bigwedge
denotes the exterior product.

Proof.

Denote
A_{\ast}:=A_{n,m,l}
, and
D_{\ast}:=D_{n,m,l}
. Let
r=\left|x\right|
. For
x=ry\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, we have
{\rm d}r=\sum_{j=1}^{d}y^{j}{\rm d}x^{j}
, and for
u\in s\Delta_{d-1}
,
s>0
,

\displaystyle{\rm d}r\wedge e^{-u_{0}A_{\ast}^{2}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d-1}\left({\rm d}A_{\ast}\right)e^{-u_{j}A_{\ast}^{2}}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{\alpha\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}^{d-1}}y^{j}\epsilon_{\left(j,\alpha\right)}e^{-uA_{\ast}^{2}}\shuffle\left(\partial_{\alpha_{1}}^{E}A_{\ast},\ldots,\partial_{\alpha_{d-1}}^{E}A_{\ast}\right){\rm d}x.

(325)

If we apply
\iota_{{\rm d}r^{\sharp}}
, the interior multiplication with the radial field, to both sides, and restrict to
S_{R}\left(0\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle e^{-u_{0}A_{\ast}^{2}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d-1}\left({\rm d}A_{\ast}\right)e^{-u_{j}A_{\ast}^{2}}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{\alpha\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}^{d-1}}y^{j}\epsilon_{\left(j,\alpha\right)}e^{-uA_{\ast}^{2}}\shuffle\left(\partial_{\alpha_{1}}^{E}A_{\ast},\ldots,\partial_{\alpha_{d-1}}^{E}A_{\ast}\right){\rm d}S_{R}\left(x\right).

(326)

We insert (
326
) into Lemma
6.4
, and change variables in the
u
-integration,

\displaystyle X_{t}:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}_{\ast}D_{\ast}}-e^{-tD_{\ast}D^{\ast}_{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}^{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\kappa_{c}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{0}^{t}s^{\frac{d}{2}-1}\int_{S_{R}\left(0\right)}A_{\ast}\int_{u\in\Delta_{d-1}}e^{-su_{0}A_{\ast}^{2}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d-1}\left({\rm d}A_{\ast}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\ast}^{2}}{\rm d}u\ {\rm d}s\right).

(327)

Due to the limit
R\to\infty
, we may replace
A_{\ast}
by
A_{\phi,\ast}:=A_{0}+\left(1-\phi\right)\left(A_{\ast}-A_{0}\right)
, for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, in (
6.1
). We note that the integrand is Bochner integrable in trace-class, since
{\rm d}A_{\ast}
is finite rank. We apply Stokes Theorem, and the trace
\mathrm{tr}_{H}
, and get

\displaystyle X_{t}=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}^{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\kappa_{c}\int_{0}^{t}s^{\frac{d}{2}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\omega_{s}^{1}+\omega_{s}^{2}\right){\rm d}s,

(328)

where

\displaystyle\omega_{s}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{u\in\Delta_{d-1}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,\ast}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}}\right){\rm d}u,

\displaystyle\omega_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle:=-2s\int_{u\in\Delta_{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}e^{-su_{0}A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d}\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,\ast}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}}\right){\rm d}u

\displaystyle=-2s\int_{u\in\Delta_{d-1}}u_{0}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,\ast}\right)e^{-su_{0}A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}}A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{d-1}\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,\ast}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}}\right){\rm d}u.

(329)

by cyclically commuting the factor
A_{\phi,\ast}^{2}
under the trace and changing variables, we obtain for
\omega_{s}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\omega_{s}^{2}=\frac{2}{d}s\partial_{s}\omega_{s}^{1}.

(330)

Integrating by parts in (
328
), we thus obtain,

\displaystyle X_{t}=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\omega^{1}_{t}.

(331)

The argument of the trace of the
n,m,l
-dependent
\omega_{t}^{1}
possesses iteratively integrable dominants in trace-norm. Indeed, first for fixed
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C
independent of
l
(because
K_{m}
is finite rank), such that

\displaystyle\left\|\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,n,m,l}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\phi,n,m,l}^{2}}\right)\left(x\right)\right\|_{S^{1}\left(H\right)}\leq C\left\|\delta_{l}\left(K_{m}P_{n}BP_{n}K_{m}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}^{d}\langle x\rangle^{-d-\epsilon}{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma \ref{radialconvolutionlem}}}}{{\leq}}C^{\prime}\left\|K_{m}P_{n}BP_{n}K_{m}\right\|_{\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon}}^{d}\langle x\rangle^{-d-\epsilon}{\rm d}x,

(332)

which is an integrable dominant on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Delta_{d-1}
, and thus allows us to take the limit
l\to\infty
under the integral.

Now fix
n
. Since
B\left(x_{0}\right)=A\left(x_{0}\right)-A_{0}=0
, the operator function
\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}B\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}
is Lipschitz with values in
S^{d}\left(H\right)
, for
\alpha,\beta
as in Hypothesis
2.11
. Since
\nabla\phi
is compactly supported it follows that
\left(\nabla\phi\right)P_{n}BP_{n}\in C_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)
. Hence there is a constant
C
independent of
m
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nu^{E}\left(\left(1-\phi\right)K_{m}P_{n}BK_{m}P_{n}\right)\left(x\right)\right\|_{S^{d}\left(H\right)}\leq C\langle x\rangle^{-1-\eta},

(333)

where
\nu
is a vector field,
\eta\geq 0
, and
\eta=\epsilon
for
\nu=\partial_{R}
. Thus

\displaystyle\left\|\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,n,m}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\phi,n,m}^{2}}\right)\left(x\right)\right\|_{S^{1}\left(H\right)}\leq C^{d}\langle x\rangle^{-d-\epsilon}{\rm d}x,

(334)

which is integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Delta_{d-1}
, which allows us to take the limit
m\to\infty
under the integral.

For the last limit
n\to\infty
, we partition
\Delta_{d-1}
via
\Delta_{d-1}=\bigcup_{k=0}^{d-1}M_{k}=\dot{\bigcup}_{k=0}^{d-1}N_{k}
, where
M_{k}:=\left\{u\in\Delta_{d-1},u_{k}\geq\frac{1}{d}\right\}
,
N_{k}:=M_{k}\backslash\left(\bigcup_{l=0}^{k-1}M_{l}\right)
. For
u\in N_{k}
, we have by Hypothesis
2.11
a constant
C
independent of
n
, such that for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi,n}\right)e^{-su_{j}A_{\phi,n}^{2}}\right)\right\|_{S^{1}\left(H\right)}\leq C\prod_{j=0}^{d-1}\tau^{\alpha,\beta_{j},\nu_{j},d,\delta_{j}}\left(\left(1-\phi\right)B\right)\sup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\langle\lambda\rangle^{-d\alpha}e^{-tu_{k}\lambda^{2}}\right)\langle x\rangle^{-d-\epsilon}{\rm d}x,

(335)

where
\beta_{j}=j\alpha
,
\nu_{j}=\nabla
for
j\neq k
,
\nu_{k}=\partial_{R}
, and
\delta_{j}=1
for
j\neq k
, and
\delta_{k}=1+\epsilon
. In the estimate we used that
P_{n}
commutes with
A_{0}
, and, as seen before, that
\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta_{j}}\left(\nu_{j}\phi\right)B\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta_{j}}\in C_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)
, which implies that the involved semi-norms
\tau^{\bullet}\left(\left(1-\phi\right)B\right)
are finite. We may interchange the limit
n\to\infty
with the trace and the integrals integrals in (
331
) and obtain the claimed formula (
6.5
).
∎

If the one-forms
{\rm d}A
admit radial limits, we may pass to these limits in the above trace formula.

Corollary 6.6
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
, and denote
A^{\circ}\left(y\right)\psi:=\lim_{R\to\infty}A\left(Ry\right)\psi
, for
\psi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, and
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
. Assume that
A^{\circ}\psi
is differentiable on
S_{1}\left(0\right)
for
\psi\in\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
, such that for a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R\left\|\partial_{i}A^{\circ}\left(y\right)\psi-\partial_{i}A\left(Ry\right)\psi\right\|_{H}=0.

(336)

and that for
\beta\in\left[-2N+\alpha,2N+1\right]
, and all unit vector fields
\nu
in
TS_{1}\left(0\right)
,

\displaystyle\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(\nu A^{\circ}\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\in L^{\infty}\left(S_{1}\left(0\right),S^{d}\left(H\right)\right).

(337)

Denote for
\rho\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle:=A_{0}+\left(1-\rho\left(\left|x\right|\right)\right)\left(A^{\circ}\left(\frac{x}{\left|x\right|}\right)-A_{0}\right).

(338)

Then for
t>0
, and any
\rho\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
, with
\rho\equiv 1
near
0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}\right)e^{-ts_{j}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}^{2}}\right){\rm d}u.

(339)

Proof.

We first note that
A^{\circ}
is well-defined. Indeed, for a.e.
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
,

\displaystyle A\left(Ry\right)-A\left(0\right)=\int_{0}^{R}\left(\partial_{R}A\right)\left(sy\right){\rm d}s,

(340)

and by Hypothesis
2.11
there is a constant
C
, such that for
\beta\in\left[-2N+\alpha,2N+1\right]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(\partial_{R}A\right)\left(sy\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{S^{d}\left(H\right)}\leq C\langle s\rangle^{-1-\epsilon},

(341)

which is integrable on
s\in\left(0,\infty\right)
. Moreover we estimate for
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
, and
k\in\left[-2N,2N\right]
by Hypothesis
2.11
,

\displaystyle\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{k}\left(A^{\circ}\left(y\right)-A_{0}\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-k}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{B\left(H\right)}\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{k}\left(A\left(x\right)-A_{0}\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-k}\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-1}_{z}\right\|_{B\left(H\right)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{z\to\infty}0,

(342)

which implies that
A^{\circ}\left(y\right)
is self-adjoint on
\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}
and that
\mathrm{Dom}\ \left(A^{\circ}\left(y\right)\right)^{k}=\mathrm{Dom}\ A_{0}^{k}
, for
k\in\left[0,2N\right]
, and that the graph norms are equivalent, uniformly in
y\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
by Kato-Rellich. Similarly we introduce for
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}:=A_{0}+\delta_{l}\left(K_{m}P_{n}\left(\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}-A_{0}\right)P_{n}K_{m}\right).

(343)

Analogously to (
6.1
) we conclude that the graph norms of
\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}^{k}
and
A_{0}^{k}
are equivalent, uniformly in
n,m,l\in\mathbb{N}
for
k\in\left[0,2N\right]
. In the proof of Theorem
6.5
we replace, due to the limit
R\to\infty
, in (
6.1
), the terms
A_{\ast}
by
\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}
and
{\rm d}A_{\ast}
by
{\rm d}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}
, which follows from the additional condition (
336
), and dominated convergence after scaling coordinates from
S_{R}\left(0\right)
to
S_{1}\left(0\right)
. The presence of
K_{m}
ensures that the limit
R\to\infty
of the integrand is still in trace-class. We now proceed similarly to the proof of Theorem
6.5
, and obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d},H\right)}\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tD^{\ast}D}-e^{-tDD^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{m\to\infty}\lim_{l\to\infty}\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}\right)e^{-ts_{j}\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}^{2}}\right){\rm d}u.

(344)

It remains to evaluate the iterated limits
n,m,l\to\infty
. Since the graph norms of the powers of
\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{n,m,l,\rho}
are uniformly equivalent to those of
A_{0}
, the limiting procedure is similar to the one in the proof of Theorem
6.5
, if we show that the operator family
\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}
satisfies for
\beta\in\left[-2N+\alpha,2N+1\right]
,

\displaystyle\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\nabla,d,1}\left(\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}-A_{0}\right)<

\displaystyle\infty,

\displaystyle\exists\epsilon>0:\tau^{\alpha,\beta,\partial_{R},d,1+\epsilon}\left(\widetilde{A^{\circ}}_{\rho}-A_{0}\right)<

\displaystyle\infty.

(345)

Indeed, by (
337
) we have a constant
C
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\alpha+\beta}\left(A^{\circ}\left(\frac{x}{\left|x\right|}\right)-A_{0}\right)\langle A_{0}\rangle^{-\beta}\right\|_{S^{d}\left(H\right)}\leq C.

(346)

The fact that
\rho^{\prime}
is compactly supported, and condition (
337
) imply by direct calculation (
6.1
) for any choice of
\epsilon>0
.
∎

7
Applications

The principal trace formulas displayed in Theorem
6.5
and in Corollary
6.6
enable us to establish a condition of the existence, and a formula for the index of
D
. At this stage we introduce the following regularized notion of an index, which might still exist even if
D
fails to be Fredholm, which is called the (partial) Witten index. Without the presence of the partial trace, its notion was introduced in
[
27
]
.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
T
be a linear, densely defined operator
\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes X
for some separable complex Hilbert space
X
. We say the partial (semi-group regularized) Witten index of
T
exists, if for some
t_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tT^{\ast}T}-e^{-tTT^{\ast}}\right)\in S^{1}\left(X\right),\ t\geq t_{0},

(347)

and if the limit

\displaystyle\ind_{W}T:=\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{tr}_{X}\left(\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tT^{\ast}T}-e^{-tTT^{\ast}}\right)\right),

(348)

exists. Then
\ind_{W}T
is called the partial Witten index of
T
.

Remark 7.2
.

Similar to the proof of
[
13
]
[Lemma 1], one obtains the analogous result for the partial Witten index. Namely, if for some
t_{0}>0
, the condition

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(e^{-tT^{\ast}T}-e^{-tTT^{\ast}}\right)\in S^{1}\left(X\right),\ t\geq t_{0}

(349)

holds, and
T
is additionally Fredholm, then
T
admits a partial Witten index,
\ind_{W}T
, which coincides with the Fredholm index
\ind T=\dim\ker T-\dim\coker T
.

It is clear that
\ind_{W}D
exists, whenever the right hand sides of the principal trace formulas in Theorem
6.5
or Corollary
6.6
admit a limit for
t\to\infty
. If
D
is Fredholm, this is the case by Remark
7.2
. However for the interesting case where
D
is non-Fredholm, we also present an example for which the Witten index exists, and can be calculated directly in terms of its symbol. We start with the Fredholm result first.

Theorem 7.3
.

Assume Hypothesis
2.11
. Additionally assume that there exists
R_{0}\geq 0
, such that for
\left|x\right|\geq R_{0}
, we have
0\in\rho\left(A\left(x\right)\right)
. Then
D
is Fredholm, and the Fredholm index
\ind D
is given by

\displaystyle\ind_{W}D=\frac{2}{d}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{i}^{d}\kappa_{c}\lim_{t\to\infty}t^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{s\in\Delta_{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\bigwedge_{j=0}^{d-1}\left(\left({\rm d}A_{\phi}\right)e^{-ts_{j}A_{\phi}^{2}}\right){\rm d}s,

(350)

where
A_{\phi}:=A_{0}+\left(1-\phi\right)\left(A-A_{0}\right)
for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
, and the formula is independent of the choice of
\phi
.

Proof.

If
D
is Fredholm,
D
admits a partial Witten index, according to Remark
7.2
, and it equals the right hand side of (
350
) by Theorem
6.5
. We thus only need to show that
D
is Fredholm. Since
A\left(x\right)
is invertible for
\left|x\right|
large enough, let
C>0
be a constant such that
A\left(x\right)^{2}\geq C
, for
\left|x\right|\geq R_{0}
. Note that
D^{\ast}D=\Delta+A^{2}-\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
as differential operator. On the domain of
D^{\ast}D
, we thus have some constant
C^{\prime}
, such that

\displaystyle D^{\ast}D\geq\Delta+C-\frac{C^{\prime}}{\langle X\rangle},

(351)

where we used that
A^{2}\geq 0
, and
\mathbbm{i}\left(\not{\partial}B\right)
is bounded, and decays like
\left|x\right|^{-1}
for
\left|x\right|\to\infty
. By Seeley’s Theorem (
[
13
]
[Theorem 1]), the elliptic differential operator
\Delta+C-\frac{C^{\prime}}{\langle X\rangle}
is Fredholm. The operator inequality (
351
) then implies that
D^{\ast}D
is also Fredholm. The analogous argument works for
DD^{\ast}
as well, hence
D
is Fredholm.
∎

The following examples are presented in more detail in
[
24
]
, here we merely quote the results without the proofs. The basic object is a Dirac-Schrödinger operator
D_{V}
which is non-Fredholm.

Definition 7.4
.

For a potential
V:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow B_{sa}\left(H\right)
, define the associated
massless
\left(d+1\right)
-Dirac-Schrödinger operator

D_{V}
,

\displaystyle\left(D_{V}f\right)\left(x,y\right):=

\displaystyle i\sum_{j=1}^{d}c^{j}\partial_{x^{j}}f\left(x,y\right)+i\partial_{y}f\left(x,y\right)+V\left(x,y\right)f\left(x,y\right),\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ y\in\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle f\in

\displaystyle W^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d+1},\mathbb{C}^{r}\otimes H\right),

(352)

Example 7.5

(
[
24
]
, Theorem 1.1)
.

Let
V\in C_{b}^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R},B_{sa}\left(H\right)\right)
, where
B_{sa}\left(H\right)
is equipped with the strong operator topology. Assume furthermore that for
i\in\left\{1,\ldots,d\right\}
and
k\in\left\{0,1,2\right\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|y\mapsto\partial_{x^{i}}\partial_{y}^{k}V\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{L^{d}\left(\mathbb{R},S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=O\left(\left|x\right|^{-1}\right),\ \left|x\right|\to\infty,

\displaystyle\exists\epsilon>0:\ \left\|y\mapsto\partial_{R}\partial_{y}^{k}V\left(x,y\right)\right\|_{L^{d}\left(\mathbb{R},S^{d}\left(H\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=O\left(\left|x\right|^{-1-\epsilon}\right),\ \left|x\right|\to\infty,

(353)

and that for any
\widehat{x}\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\left|\gamma\right|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R^{\left|\gamma\right|}\left\|\left(\partial^{\gamma}_{x}V\right)\left(x_{0}+R\widehat{x},\cdot\right)-\left(\partial^{\gamma}_{x}V\right)\left(R\widehat{x},\cdot\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R},B\left(H\right)\right)}=0.

(354)

Then

\displaystyle\ind_{W}D_{V}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(4\pi\right)^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{d-1}{2}\right)!}{d!}\kappa_{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\left(U^{V}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}U^{V}\right)^{\wedge d},

(355)

where
\wedge d
is the
d
-fold exterior power,

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}:=\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbb{C}^{r}}\left(c^{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot c^{d}\right),

(356)

and the unitary
U^{V}
is given by
U^{V}\left(x\right):=\lim_{n\to\infty}U^{V\left(x,\cdot\right)}\left(n,-n\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)
,
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, for a given
A:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow B_{sa}\left(H\right)
is the (unique) evolutions system of the equation

\displaystyle u^{\prime}\left(y\right)=\mathbbm{i}A\left(y\right)u\left(y\right),\ y\in\mathbb{R},

(357)

i.e. for
y,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{i}A\left(y_{1}\right)U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right),

(358)

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{2}}U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle-\mathbbm{i}U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)A\left(y_{2}\right),

(359)

\displaystyle U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{2}\right)U^{A}\left(y_{2},y_{3}\right)=

\displaystyle U^{A}\left(y_{1},y_{3}\right),

(360)

\displaystyle U^{A}\left(y,y\right)=

\displaystyle 1_{H}.

(361)

With the minimal choice
r=2^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
, and sign convention
3
3

3

This corresponds to the choice
c^{j}=-\mathbbm{i}\sigma^{j}
, where
\sigma^{j}
are classical Pauli matrices.

\kappa_{c}=\left(-\mathbbm{i}\right)^{d}\left(2\mathbbm{i}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\ind_{W}D_{V}=\left(2\pi\mathbbm{i}\right)^{-\frac{d+1}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{d-1}{2}\right)!}{d!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{tr}_{H}\left(\left(U^{V}\right)^{-1}{\rm d}U^{V}\right)^{\wedge d}.

(362)

This formula generalizes an index formula found in
[
7
]
,
[
14
]
, and
[
16
]
for the case
d=1
to
d\geq 3
odd. For a special choice of the potential
V
, the index formula becomes even more concrete. We shall close the paper with this example.

Definition 7.6
.

Let
F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded smooth function with bounded derivatives, such that

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{i}}F\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\left|x\right|^{-1}\right),\ \left|x\right|\to\infty,

\displaystyle\exists\epsilon>0:\ \partial_{R}F\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\left|x\right|^{-1-\epsilon}\right),\ \left|x\right|\to\infty,

(363)

and that for any
\widehat{x}\in S_{1}\left(0\right)
,
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
\left|\gamma\right|\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}R^{\left|\gamma\right|}\left|\left(\partial^{\gamma}F\right)\left(x_{0}+R\widehat{x}\right)-\left(\partial^{\gamma}F\right)\left(R\widehat{x}\right)\right|=0.

(364)

Set
V\left(x,y\right):=\mathbbm{i}\sum_{j=1}^{d}c^{j}F_{j}\left(x\right)\phi\left(y\right)
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}
, for some function
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
with
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi\left(y\right){\rm d}y=1
.

Example 7.7

(
[
24
]
, Proposition 1.2)
.

With
V
as in Definition
7.6
, with the minimal choices
\mathrm{rank}\left(c^{i}\right)=2^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
and sign convention
\kappa_{c}=\left(-\mathbbm{i}\right)^{d}\left(2\mathbbm{i}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}
, the partial Witten index of
D_{V}
exists, and

\displaystyle\ind_{W}D_{V}=\left(2\pi\right)^{-\frac{d+1}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{d-1}{2}\right)!}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(\left|F\right|+d-1\right)\left(\cos\left(2\left|F\right|\right)-1\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}}{\left|F\right|^{d}}\det\mathrm{D}F\ {\rm d}x,

(365)

where
\mathrm{D}F
is the Jacobian of
F
. Note that the integrand extends continuously to
F=0
.

The above formula allows one to show that
\ind_{W}
may assume any real number for this class of operators
D_{V}
if one chooses appropriate diffeomorphisms of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
onto balls centred around
0
for the function
F
(cf.
[
24
]
[Corollary 4.6]).

8
